    jobDescription

    Ongoing and non-ongoing opportunities | Affirmative Measures – First Nations


    Lead from the front. Shape outcomes. Make a lasting difference for First Nations Communities. 


    Are you an experienced First Nations leader ready to take on a critical executive leadership role?


    AHL is seeking capable and decisive leaders to take on the role as an Executive Level 2 (EL2) Frontline Director. These are pivotal positions with responsibilities for leading complex service delivery operations, setting direction for regional teams and ensuring that AHL’s hostel network delivers safe, effective and culturally responsive services to clients and communities. 


    As a Frontline Director, you will lead from the front in a dynamic and often challenging environment. You will be required to make sound and timely decisions, navigate complex operational and people matters, manage competing priorities and take accountability for outcomes. You will work across regional boundaries, providing leadership and direction where it is needed most, while building strong relationships with First Nations communities and key stakeholders. 


    These roles require more than operational oversight. They call for leaders who can bring clarity and direction to complex situations, exercise sound judgement, build organisational capability and drive outcomes that make a genuine difference to people and communities. 
    jobDuties

    About the roles


    As a Frontline Director, you will provide critical executive leadership across AHL’s hostel network, translating organisational priorities into effective regional operations and ensuring the network responds to changing business and community needs. 


    You will be accountable for regional performance and service delivery, leading through complexity, addressing significant operational and workforce issues, and working with senior leaders to strengthen the overall performance of our hostel network. 


    Key responsibilities include: 


    • Setting direction and taking accountability for operational performance, service delivery and outcomes. 
    • Making sound decisions in complex and sensitive situations, managing risks and competing priorities.
    • Leading workforce performance, capability and accountability, including through periods of change. 
    • Managing complex client matters and ensuring services are safe, effective and culturally responsive. 
    • Building trusted relationships with First Nations communities and key stakeholders to inform decisions and improve outcomes. 
    • Driving continuous improvement and collaborating across the network to maintain service continuity and respond to emerging needs. 


    These roles require visible, engaged leadership and a willingness to travel to support your region or provide temporary coverage in other regions where necessary. 


    What we are looking for


    We are seeking experienced leaders who demonstrate: 


    • A strong record of leading complex operations, settling direction and delivering outcomes. 
    • Sound judgement and decision-making in challenging, sensitive or high-pressured environments. 
    • The ability to lead through others, build capability and foster culture of accountability and high performance. 
    • Strong strategic thinking, risk management and problem-solving skills. 
    • Highly developed stakeholder engagement and influencing skills, including the ability to build trusted relationships with First Nations communities. 
    • A commitment to culturally responsive service delivery and continuous improvement. 


    You will bring the leadership presence, resilience and integrity to take ownership of complex issues, lead from the front and deliver outcomes for clients, staff and communities.

    departmentDescription The Australian Criminal Intelligence Commission (ACIC) is Australia’s national criminal intelligence agency. Our vision is an Australia hostile to criminal exploitation. We work with our national and international partners to conduct investigations and collect intelligence to combat serious and organised crime, cybercrime and national security threats. We develop and maintain national information and intelligence sharing services and systems for use by state, territory and Commonwealth law enforcement agencies. We also deliver nationwide background checking services to support employment or entitlement decisions to keep the Australian community safe. The ACIC Board provides strategic direction to the ACIC and represents Commonwealth, state and territory law enforcement and other regulatory agencies. Our purpose is to protect Australia from criminal threats through coordinating a strategic response and collecting, assessing and disseminating intelligence and policing information.
    jobDescription


    The Opportunity


    As an ACIC Senior Investigator, you will lead the design and execution of intelligence collection plans in response to established intelligence priorities and requirements, and report the intelligence obtained through these activities. You will apply advanced collection tradecraft within authorised legislative frameworks. The role involves close collaboration with intelligence analysts at all stages of the intelligence cycle to understand intelligence gaps and requirements, inform operational planning, and to maximise the value of the intelligence obtained from collection.


    Investigators are results orientated, adept at managing risk and comfortable with partial operational autonomy. Successful applicants may be required to travel domestically or internationally. They must be able to develop productive working relationships which enhance or leverage opportunities with Law Enforcement and National Intelligence Community partners, to ensure the ACIC is positioned to deliver intelligence insights and operational outcomes.


    The investigator role requires a need to obtain special member status with the Australian Federal Police (AFP) and ability to acquire this status is an essential requirement of the position.
    jobDuties

    Role responsibilities


    As a Senior Investigator, you will undertake the following activities to a level reflecting the standards associated with the APS EL1 level: 


    • In accordance with legislative authority, plan and lead collection activity targeting serious and organised crime (SOC) threats using a range of overt and covert capabilities, including Human Source operations, coercive powers and controlled operations, to collect and report intelligence

    • Ensure appropriate operational planning and security frameworks are applied to collection capabilities and activities

    • Contribute to communities of practice to share expertise and refine collection techniques

    • Apply collection best practice to improve the effectiveness and reliability of intelligence gathering

    • Collaborate with law enforcement, National Intelligence Community and offshore partners to influence and coordinate intelligence collection efforts, including joint operations and intelligence sharing

    • Build and maintain trusted relationships with external stakeholders to support long-term collaboration and capability development

    • Consider and deploy a range of risk mitigation strategies

    • Actively manage and document compliance activities in line with prescribed obligations


    Essential Criteria


    • Successfully complete the ACIC’s human source operations training program on commencement

    • As an employee in a designated high-risk role, complete an annual psychological assessment.

    • Must be eligible to obtain special member status and use of force with the AFP. The criteria for this requirement are having previously been a sworn police officer within the past 3 years or recent military training.


    Your Skills and Capabilities


    We invite applications from people with the following experience, skills and attributes: 


    • Demonstrated understanding of investigative and/or intelligence collection and analysis methodologies

    • Experience in implementing complex intelligence operations and/or investigations that generate unique insights into SOC or similarly complex networks

    • Ability to extract relevant information from intelligence operations and investigations to produce unique and impactful information and intelligence reports

    • Ability to understand how to operate within the context of relevant legislative, policy and legal frameworks domestically and offshore

    • Highly developed oral and written communication skills, including the ability to develop and maintain collaborative and productive internal and external working relationships

    • Proven capacity to operate as part of a multi-disciplinary team in a dynamic, agile environment to drive intelligence outcomes

    • Experience in the cultivation, recruitment and management of human sources and the effective deployment of human sources again operational objectives
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    • Citizenship – To be eligible for employment with the ACIC, applicants must be an Australian citizen.

    • Security Clearance - The Australian community expects ACIC employees to maintain the highest levels of security and integrity. Successful applicants will be required to obtain and maintain a minimum Negative Vetting Level 1 security clearance.

    • Pre-Employment Screening – All candidates must satisfy an Organisational Suitability Assessment (OSA) prior to being offered employment at the ACIC. Please refer to the ACIC’s Character Standards for further information.

Executive Level 1 - Australian Institute of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander Studies - Warlpiri Assistant Director Return of Cultural Heritage (Affirmative Measures)

    Salary $121,755 to $132,713

    jobLocation Alice Springs NT, Canberra ACT

    departmentDescription We are Australia’s only national institution focused exclusively on the diverse history, cultures, and heritage of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander Australia. We house the world’s most important and extensive collection of cultural information and material and we provide leadership, publish and promote greater understanding and appreciation of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples. We create opportunities for people the world over to engage with and be transformed by the knowledge, the culture and the story of Australia’s Indigenous peoples. We are Australia’s national institution for leadership in Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander research. We care for a priceless collection, including films, photographs, video and audio recordings as well as the world’s largest collection of printed and other resource materials for Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander studies. We undertake and encourage scholarly, ethical, community-based research in a variety of sectors, including health, native title, languages and education. Our publishing house, Aboriginal Studies Press, regularly publishes outstanding writing that promotes Australian Indigenous cultures. Our activities affirm and raise awareness of the richness and diversity of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander cultures and histories.
    jobDescription

    About the Group

    The Office of the CEO (OCEO) is at the forefront of the Institute, liaising with external stakeholders including, but not limited to: Ministerial Offices, Commonwealth and state and Territory Departments; including the National Indigenous Australians Agency (NIAA), the Department of Foreign Affairs and Trade (DFaT) and the Office of the Arts (OfTA) as well as other Indigenous Portfolio Bodies, the Australian Diplomatic Corp, relevant Embassies and Cultural and other Institutions both domestically and abroad.


    About the Program Area

    AIATSIS is leading a program to secure the return of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples’ cultural heritage held overseas to its original custodians and owners or appropriate cultural institutions.

    Returning material to country for purposes of cultural revitalisation is a key aspiration of Indigenous communities. The Return of Cultural Heritage (RoCH) program supports the cultural resurgence of Australia’s First Nations peoples. This initiative will strengthen the signal both to the nation and globally that Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander culture is respected, celebrated and valued.

    The program will rely on expert knowledge as well as strong community engagement and partnerships, and a network of engaged individuals and organisations to achieve its goals.


    Role Summary

    The Warlpiri Assistant Director is responsible for the development of research strategies, conducting high-quality research and evaluation of outcomes, producing reports, official return requests, policy papers and other outputs. They are responsible for engagement with Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples, communities and organisations, as well as domestic and international cultural institutions and collectors.

    This role is funded through external (corporate) funding sources with a primary focus to work in partnership with the Warlpiri communities impacted by the Granites gold mines, supporting the delivery of community-centred initiatives, building strong relationships, and helping to advance priorities that contribute to positive social, cultural and economic outcomes.

    The position provides support to the Director in informing the legislative requirements and the development of changes to repatriation policy, practice and guidelines.
    jobDuties

    Key Responsibilities

    • developing and monitoring of work plans and workflows, the delivery of outputs, and the mentoring and guiding of team members.
    • working with other team members, develop research strategies, lead development of, and undertake, high-quality research.
    • support the Director in stakeholder engagement through preparation of research, reports and briefs, negotiations with communities and institutions, preparation of meeting notes, ensuring follow up actions and outcomes are completed. 
    • assist the Director through the preparation of written articles for a variety of publications and presentations, and the preparation of media content.
    • support and oversee administrative practices, systems and procedures to optimise efficiency and support the achievement of quality outcomes.
    • the Assistant Director is expected to both model appropriate behaviour and to contribute to the mission and objectives of AIATSIS in that capacity.
    • some domestic and international travel will be required.


    Key Challenges

    • anticipating and addressing contentious issues and providing accurate advice on complex issues while maintaining a high level of cultural competency and confidence.


    Key Relationships

    • Warlpiri communities 

    this role will work directly with Warlpiri communities impacted by the Granites gold mines. The successful candidate must have established networks within these communities and an in-depth knowledge of their culture and cultural practices.


    • Office of the CEO 

    provide expert advice and contribute to decision making; identify emerging issues/risks and their implications and propose solutions; provide advice and report on progress towards business objectives and discuss future directions; support the development and implementation of work programs.


    • Executive 

    collaborate and provide advice to contribute to strategic decision-making.


    • Director RoCH 

    support the Director in managing and supporting the tasks of the team; support with development and implementation of the strategic direction of the work program.


    • Stakeholders (internal) 

    liaise with, and coordinate information between stakeholders and the Executive; consult and collaborate with, to define mutual interests and determine strategies to achieve their realisation.


    • Stakeholders (external) 

    collaborate and negotiate in research and engagement activities, provide sound and reliable advice; manage expectations, resolve problems and provide solutions to issues; negotiate outcomes and timeframes.
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    Cultural capability - essential

    Roles at AIATSIS require candidates to have an understanding of the issues affecting Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples and proven culturally appropriate engagement and communications skills. The nature of this role requires the candidate to consistently demonstrate the following specific cultural capabilities:

    • an understanding of the AIATSIS vision to create a world in which Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people’s knowledge and cultures are recognised, respected, celebrated and valued 
    • an informed, authentic and respectful understanding of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander cultures and societies, including ethical research and policy matters related to the lives of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander people 
    • the capability to incorporate Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples’ voices, experiences and perspectives in all of our work.


    Skills, knowledge and personal attributes

    To be successful in this role, you will have established networks with Warlpiri communities and an in-depth knowledge of their culture and cultural practices. You will come with the following skills, experience and attributes: 

    • be a highly culturally proficient individual with practical experience with Warlpiri communities, Indigenous engagement, with a demonstrated capacity to engage and negotiate in good faith, with the intent to benefit Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples, or a demonstrated capacity to develop this experience.
    • have a strong track record of research experience, with demonstrated capacity to lead and build research/curatorial/policy projects.
    • be a clear and effective communicator, with emotional intelligence and self-awareness, and a creative thinker. You will have excellent writing and analytical skills and the ability to communicate ideas clearly to a wide range of audiences.
    • possess demonstrated project management skills, including the ability to plan, monitor and evaluate projects, budgets, time frames and seeing projects through to completion.
    • have the ability to model ethical and appropriate behaviour, to provide leadership, build collaborations and partnerships, and develop relations with Indigenous communities and other stakeholders, including public representation.
    • experience or qualifications in research/cultural heritage management/cultural collections or repatriation, or in the repatriation environment (legal, policy, cultural) dealing with Aboriginal and Torres Strait cultural heritage, is highly desirable.

    Affirmative Measures positions

    Jobs in the APS are open to all Australian citizens. There are some jobs, though, that are open only to specific members of the Australian population. This practice is known as an Affirmative measure and is a targeted strategy to ensure the diversity of the APS workforce reflects the diversity of the Australian population.  


    Any job can be restricted to Aboriginal and/or Torres Strait Islander people only, and it doesn't necessarily have to involve working on Indigenous issues. If a position has been advertised as an Affirmative measure, it will be clearly marked as such.


    The use of the Indigenous Affirmative Measures provision at AIATSIS allows for targeted recruitment of Indigenous Australians into the organisation. If you are applying for an Affirmative Measures position, you will need to indicate your Aboriginal and/or Torres Strait Islander heritage on our application form and provide confirmation if required. 


    What is Cultural Capability? 

    As all positions at AIATSIS are ‘Identified’ each position will have various elements of involvement with the delivery of Indigenous specific services, programs, and policies as part of AIATSIS core functions. AIATSIS therefore needs to also identify the cultural knowledge; skills and personal attributes needed to deliver the required outcomes.


    Every part of how we work, who we work with and for, requires varying levels of knowledge of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander cultures, protocols and an understanding of issues that enable effective communication and ultimately effective performance in the role. To assist you in determining the required cultural capabilities of this role, you can refer to the Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander Cultural Capability – A Framework for Commonwealth Agencies

    The Organisation

    The Australian Security Intelligence Organisation (ASIO) protects Australia and Australians from threats to their security. ASIO’s people are ordinary Australians but they do extraordinary things – they are our most important asset. To be successful, we need talented people who are highly capable, dedicated, adaptable and resilient.


    We seek to reflect the community we protect. ASIO is committed to fostering a diverse and inclusive environment, where all staff are valued and respected. We welcome applications from all eligible candidates, irrespective of gender, sexual orientation, ethnicity, religious affiliation, age or disability. Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples are encouraged to apply. We are secretive about what we do, not what we value.


    At a time when Australia’s security environment is dynamic, diverse and degrading, our work is more important than ever. Join the mission and make a difference every day.

    The opportunity

    ASIO is seeking proactive, motivated and passionate Business Analysts. You will contribute to ASIO’s mission through working with project managers, technical experts and business stakeholders at all levels to investigate, analyse, validate, and document business, functional and non-functional requirements. You will guide technology change through all stages of the project lifecycle and play a key role in transitioning ASIO staff to new ways of working.


    You will have the ability to drive project requirements from inception to delivery. As a pivotal part of cross-functional multi-disciplinary teams, you will use a broad range of business analysis techniques to:

    ·        Define business problems

    ·        Understand business teams’ current state

    ·        Define future state

    ·        Design and prototype user interfaces

    ·        Document business, functional and non-functional requirements

    ·        Lead user acceptance testing

    ·        Support technical and business teams through implementation and adoption.


    A merit pool may be created to fill future vacancies which have the same or similar requirements to this position. This merit pool will be valid for up to 18 months.
    jobDuties

    Responsibilities

    As a Business Analyst, you will:

    ·        Investigate business needs, problems and opportunities to support and recommend effective business and ICT changes.

    ·        Develop artefacts to support the requirements definition such as requirement specifications, user stories, process models, operational concepts, prototypes and data driven insights.

    ·        Build relationships and work collaboratively with stakeholders by facilitating workshops,

    stakeholder interviews, presentations, product backlog management and user acceptance

    testing.

    ·        Contribute to and manage activities for planning, development, enhancement and

    implementation of projects and systems.

    ·        Identify issues, risks and benefits of current and proposed solutions and apply expertise to

    detail the resulting impacts.

    ·        Lead and support business transition to embed change.

    ·        Continually develop the business analysis competency within the Organisation.


    ITE2 Business Analyst (APS6 equivalent): The ITE2 role will be capable of working under broad direction to perform and achieve a range of moderately complex ICT business analysis work in a variety of contexts.


    SITEC Senior Business Analyst (EL1 equivalent): The SITEC role will be capable of working under limited direction to perform and achieve highly complex ICT business analysis work in a wide and sometimes unpredictable range of contexts. This is a leadership role and may include line management of staff.
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    What you will bring

    We invite applications from people with the following attributes:

    ·        Business analysis skills and experience in software development, project management and agile frameworks and methodologies.

    ·        Ability to develop and apply business analysis deliverables, tools and techniques in a range of contexts.

    ·        Experience in developing stakeholder and client engagement at a variety of levels.

    What we offer you

    ASIO provides a number of benefits to its staff including:

    ·        A competitive salary, including a 7.5% allowance for maintaining a TOP SECRET-Privileged Access (TS-PA) security clearance.

    ·        Employer superannuation contributions of 15.4%.

    ·        Access to 7 staff-led diversity and inclusion networks.

    ·        Access to an Employee Assistance Program (EAP).


    Location

    These positions are located in Canberra. Relocation assistance is provided to successful candidates where ASIO requires you to relocate.

    We advertise positions nationally to reach the broadest audience. Please refer to the above location before applying.


    How to apply

    ASIO officers’ identities are protected by law. Under section 92 of the ASIO Act, the only declared ASIO officers are the Director-General and Deputy Directors-General. Due to the need to protect our people’s identities, ASIO holds all employment applications in the strictest of confidence. It is essential that you do the same. Please do not discuss your application with others as doing so may adversely affect your potential employment.


    Click on ‘Apply online’ to commence your application. Your application must be complete and include the following:

    ·        A written pitch of up to 500 words that addresses the required capabilities for the role. Use examples to demonstrate how your skills and experience meet the capabilities.

    ·        A current CV, no more than 2 pages in length, outlining your employment history, the dates and a brief description of your role, as well as any academic qualifications or relevant training you have undertaken.

    ·        Details of 2 referees, which must include a current or former supervisor. ASIO will not contact your referees without advising you prior to doing so.


    Before applying read the ASIO People Capability Framework and Core Capability Guide, both available on the ASIO website. To increase your chances of success, tailor your application responses to align with the relevant capabilities and levels outlined in these frameworks.


    Use of artificial intelligence in recruitment processes

    ASIO does not use any form of artificial intelligence (AI) at any point in the recruitment process.

    All applications are reviewed by a selection panel. We recognise some applicants may use AI technologies to draft applications or prepare for interviews. If you wish to use AI, we recommend doing this only for initial drafts and ensure that your application is personalised and accurately reflects your skills, experience and qualifications.


    Additionally, be aware of the privacy policies of any AI tool you use. Be mindful of the information you are entering into AI tools (personal details, work history, etc) and how using it may link you to potential ASIO employment, noting that you are expected to be discrete and maintain confidentiality with your application.


    If you are invited to attend interview, the use of AI tools and recording devices during interview is strictly prohibited. Should AI tools or recording services be detected, your application, and any future applications may be discontinued.

    The recruitment selection process

    Our selection process is rigorous and extensive. All employment decisions and selection processes at ASIO are based on merit principles and candidates must be prepared to undergo various selection stages. The stages may include application assessment, eligibility checks, online testing, skills-based assessment and interview.


    We ask all applicants for their patience throughout the process. 

    Employment conditions

    Employment is under the Australian Security Intelligence Organisation Act 1979. Conditions of service are similar to those applying in the Australian Public Service. We recognise entitlements accrued under the Public Service Act 1999 and provide for continuation of superannuation under the Commonwealth schemes. Salary packaging arrangements are also available.

    Australian Workplace Equality Index

    ASIO has achieved the Australian Workplace Equality Index (AWEI) Gold Standard in 2024, 2025 and 2026, recognising ASIO’s inclusion work and positive culture. ASIO was the first Australian intelligence agency to achieve this status.

    ﻿

    For more information about ASIO, please visit: www.asio.gov.au.

Executive Level 1 - National Anti-Corruption Commission - Senior Investigator

    departmentDescription The National Anti-Corruption Commission (NACC) is an independent Commonwealth agency that commenced in 2023. The NACC mission is to enhance integrity in the Commonwealth public sector by deterring, detecting and preventing corrupt conduct involving Commonwealth public officials through education, monitoring, investigation, reporting and referral.
    jobDescription

    About the National Anti-Corruption Commission


    The NACC enhances integrity in the Commonwealth public sector by deterring, detecting, and preventing corrupt conduct involving Commonwealth public officials. It does this through education, monitoring, investigation, reporting and referral.


    Working with us

    This is an exciting time to join the NACC. We are looking for outstanding individuals to lead our corruption investigations and deliver timely outcomes to enhance integrity in the public service.


    NACC staff work in a complex and dynamic setting where exceptional judgement and an ability to achieve results is critical.


    Successful candidates will have the ability to cooperate, manage, lead and perform across corporate and operational functions.


    About the Role


    Reporting to the Director Operations and under broad guidance, the Senior Investigator is responsible for the conduct of complex investigations into allegations of corrupt conduct, using the full range of investigative methodologies, including coercive powers available under the National Anti-Corruption Commission Act 2022 (Cth) (NACC Act). 
    jobDuties

    In accordance with the Executive Level 1 Work Level Standards, you will:


    • Lead the planning and ongoing management of corruption investigations, including record keeping, to ensure robust and timely outcomes.
    • Facilitate effective working relationships within a multidisciplinary team environment, comprising investigators, intelligence analysts, lawyers, and other specialists.
    • Undertake and/or assist in all relevant investigative activities, including: 
      • identify relevant lines of enquiry and prepare investigation plans for complex investigations.
      • collect evidence including drafting decision minutes and NACC Act notices, preparing affidavits to obtain warrants, and conducting interviews.
      • oversee the analysis of large and complex data sets of information. 
      • preparing investigation reports, obtaining witness statements, hearing briefs and briefs of evidence, and giving evidence in court.
    • Provide advice to the Commissioner and senior executives of the NACC in relation to the ongoing progress and outcomes of corruption investigations. 
    • Ensure effective working relationships and governance of investigations being jointly undertaken with partner agencies.
    • Represent the interests of the NACC and helping manage the NACC’s relationships with relevant stakeholders including other Australian Government agencies, State and Territory representatives and the private sector as necessary.
    • Lead by example and develop, coach and mentor more junior staff. This may include direct line management of APS6 investigators.
    • Undertake and contribute to the continuous improvement of the investigative function within the NACC, including; developing, reviewing, evaluating and implementing relevant policies, procedures, processes, tools and best practices.
    • Lead and exemplify a healthy and safe working environment, modelling and promoting ethical behaviour and practices consistent with the APS Code of Conduct. 
    • On occasion, undertake travel throughout Australia (including regional and remote areas), as operationally required. 


    The NACC is a small agency. In addition to these activities, from time to time you may be asked to undertake tasks that are outside your usual span of duties but that are within the range of your capabilities. Supporting one another in this way provides opportunities for exposure across the agency and is an integral part of the NACC’s positive workplace culture.   
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    Employment with the NACC is subject to the following conditions:


    Citizenship – Applicants must be an Australian Citizen.

    Security Clearance – This is a Designated Security Assessment Position (DSAP). The occupant of this position must obtain and retain an AGSVA security clearance to the specified level.

    Suitability assessment – The NACC must be satisfied that successful applicants are of suitable character. They must undergo a rigorous pre-employment suitability screening process that can be intrusive in nature including (but not limited to) detailed background, character, employment, police, and financial checks.

    Probation - A probation period will apply to any new ongoing engagement to the Australian Public Service.



    Eligibility (Prescribed Qualifications)/Other Requirements:


    • Demonstrated experience in a similar role.
    • Diploma in Government Investigations (or the ability to obtain within 12 months of commencement).
    • Legal background will be highly regarded but not essential.
    • Undertake any necessary training as required from time to time pertaining to the exercising of powers as an Authorised Officer under the NACC Act or other requirements of the role.
    • Due to the nature of the role, you may at times be exposed to objectionable/undesirable material, either directly or indirectly and must be aware of this possibility.
    • You may be required to occasionally work outside of the core hours, including weekends.
    • Given the nature of the NACCs operational activities, employees may be involved in matters where their identity or involvement becomes know to affected persons, witnesses or external stakeholders. Employees should be aware that anonymity cannot be guaranteed, notwithstanding the NACCs commitment to managing employee safety, security and wellbeing risks.
    jobCategory Intelligence
    jobContact Nigel
    jobContactPhone (02) 9797 3815

    • an additional 3 days paid leave between Christmas and New Year.

    • an option to purchase additional leave, up to 4 weeks per year

    • access to studies assistance.

This includes the need for high-level personal security assessments, the intrusion associated with notifying private financial and social interests and the necessity to engage in practices to prevent targeting by unlawful elements.

    departmentDescription The Bureau of Meteorology is one of the few organisations that touches the lives of all Australians and all Australia, every day. The Bureau works across Australia and remote islands, providing services from the Antarctic to beyond the equator, and from the Indian Ocean to the Pacific. We are Australia's national weather, climate and water agency, in the Agriculture, Water and Environment portfolio of the Australian Government, operating under the authority of the Meteorology Act 1955 and the Water Act 2007. We provide data, information, knowledge, insight and wisdom to help Australians prepare and respond to the realities of their natural environment, including droughts, floods, fires, storms, tsunami and tropical cyclones. Our products and services include observations, forecasts, analysis and advice covering Australia's atmosphere, water, oceans and space environments. We undertake focused scientific research in support of our operations and services. Through regular forecasts, warnings, monitoring and advice, we provide one of Australia's most fundamental and widely used public services. We have strong relationships with our customers, partners and stakeholders in Australia, including the Australian Community and the emergency services sectors, all-levels of Government, and focus sectors including aviation, agriculture, energy and resources, national security and water.
    jobDescription

    The Bureau's Enterprise Portfolio Management Office (EPMO) sits in the Strategy Program within the Enterprise Services Group. The EPMO provides whole of enterprise coordination, strategic guidance and support across the Bureau’s project, program, and portfolio management capabilities.


    This role supports the EPMO Manager by developing and maintaining the processes and standards that; control new work entering the Bureau's portfolio, support the monitoring and control of active projects; and enable executive level oversight of portfolio performance.  


    The successful candidate will be a capable and experienced leader with significant experience managing a portfolio and enabling strategic decision-making processes. The candidate will demonstrate an in-depth knowledge of Portfolio Management and project delivery methods and possess well developed communication and interpersonal skills. They will show initiative, possess excellent problem-solving and negotiation skills and will have a fine eye for quality and detail.


    This position reports to the EPMO Manager. 
    jobDuties

    The responsibilities of the role include but are not limited to:


    1. Lead the development, optimisation and governance of the Bureau's enterprise portfolio in collaboration with the Bureau's Finance team and investment decision making committee, ensuring investment decisions are strategically aligned and within available capacity. 

    2. Oversee the Bureau's project pipeline process and required governance approvals to control new work coming into the Bureau's portfolio. 

    3. Build a strong network with General Manager (SES band 1) stakeholders to provide strategic advice on investment prioritisation, portfolio performance, benefits realisation, delivery risks and organisational capacity, supporting informed enterprise decision-making.

    4. Lead engagement with the Bureau's Finance Team to support high level project capacity planning and resource management.

    5. Lead the development, application, and continual improvement of the EPMO's Portfolio Management Framework and supporting artefacts.

    6. Lead the continuous improvement of the Bureau's Benefits Management Framework ensuring benefits are identified, measured, realised and reported to support strategic decision-making and investment prioritisation. 

    7. Provide strategic input to the development of New Policy Proposals, Business Cases and other large scale funding bids.

    8. Develop portfolio reports and high level insights for the Bureau's Executive Team and senior decision making forums.

    9. Manage EPMO centralised registers that contain information inputs required to support portfolio management.

    10. Active involvement in the continual improvement of all processes across EPMO services.

    11. Comply with all Bureau work, health and safety policies and procedures, and take reasonable care for your own health and safety and that of employees, contractors and visitors who may be affected by your conduct.

    12. Commitment to APS Values, Employment Principles, Code of Conduct, and the Bureau's Social Justice Strategy.
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    A degree or diploma of an Australian educational institution, or a comparable overseas qualification, which is appropriate to the duties; OR other comparable qualifications, which are appropriate to the duties.

Executive Level 1 - Australian Criminal Intelligence Commission (ACIC) - Senior Investigator
    Field Data

    Salary $121,756 to $146,676

    departmentDescription The Australian Criminal Intelligence Commission (ACIC) is Australia’s national criminal intelligence agency. Our vision is an Australia hostile to criminal exploitation. We work with our national and international partners to conduct investigations and collect intelligence to combat serious and organised crime, cybercrime and national security threats. We develop and maintain national information and intelligence sharing services and systems for use by state, territory and Commonwealth law enforcement agencies. We also deliver nationwide background checking services to support employment or entitlement decisions to keep the Australian community safe. The ACIC Board provides strategic direction to the ACIC and represents Commonwealth, state and territory law enforcement and other regulatory agencies. Our purpose is to protect Australia from criminal threats through coordinating a strategic response and collecting, assessing and disseminating intelligence and policing information.
    jobDescription


    The Opportunity


    As an ACIC Senior Investigator, you will lead the design and execution of intelligence collection plans in response to established intelligence priorities and requirements, and report the intelligence obtained through these activities. You will apply advanced collection tradecraft within authorised legislative frameworks. The role involves close collaboration with intelligence analysts at all stages of the intelligence cycle to understand intelligence gaps and requirements, inform operational planning, and to maximise the value of the intelligence obtained from collection.


    Investigators are results orientated, adept at managing risk and comfortable with partial operational autonomy. Successful applicants may be required to travel domestically or internationally. They must be able to develop productive working relationships which enhance or leverage opportunities with Law Enforcement and National Intelligence Community partners, to ensure the ACIC is positioned to deliver intelligence insights and operational outcomes.


    The ACIC is undergoing legislation change. Investigator roles may require a need to obtain special member status (exercising powers of a police constable) in the medium term; however, this should not prohibit interest in these roles at the current time, despite it being listed in the essential criteria.
    jobDuties

    Role Responsibilities


    As a Senior Investigator, you will undertake the following activities to a level reflecting the standards associated with the APS EL1 level: 


    • In accordance with legislative authority, plan and lead collection activity targeting serious and organised crime (SOC) threats using a range of overt and covert capabilities, including Human Source operations, coercive powers and controlled operations, to collect and report intelligence

    • Ensure appropriate operational planning and security frameworks are applied to collection capabilities and activities

    • Contribute to communities of practice to share expertise and refine collection techniques

    • Apply collection best practice to improve the effectiveness and reliability of intelligence gathering

    • Collaborate with law enforcement, National Intelligence Community and offshore partners to influence and coordinate intelligence collection efforts, including joint operations and intelligence sharing

    • Build and maintain trusted relationships with external stakeholders to support long-term collaboration and capability development

    • Consider and deploy a range of risk mitigation strategies

    • Actively manage and document compliance activities in line with prescribed obligations


    Essential Criteria


    • Successfully complete the ACIC’s human source operations training program on commencement

    • As an employee in a designated high-risk role, complete an annual psychological assessment.

    • Must be eligible to obtain special member status and use of force with the AFP. The criteria for this requirement are having previously been a sworn police officer within the past 3 years or recent military training.


    Your Skills and Capabilities


    We invite applications from people with the following experience, skills and attributes: 


    • Demonstrated understanding of investigative and/or intelligence collection and analysis methodologies

    • Experience in implementing complex intelligence operations and/or investigations that generate unique insights into SOC or similarly complex networks

    • Ability to extract relevant information from intelligence operations and investigations to produce unique and impactful information and intelligence reports

    • Ability to understand how to operate within the context of relevant legislative, policy and legal frameworks domestically and offshore

    • Highly developed oral and written communication skills, including the ability to develop and maintain collaborative and productive internal and external working relationships

    • Proven capacity to operate as part of a multi-disciplinary team in a dynamic, agile environment to drive intelligence outcomes

    • Experience in the cultivation, recruitment and management of human sources and the effective deployment of human sources again operational objectives
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    • Citizenship – To be eligible for employment with the ACIC, applicants must be an Australian citizen.

    • Security Clearance - The Australian community expects ACIC employees to maintain the highest levels of security and integrity. Successful applicants will be required to obtain and maintain a minimum Negative Vetting Level 1 security clearance.

    • Pre-Employment Screening – All candidates must satisfy an Organisational Suitability Assessment (OSA) prior to being offered employment at the ACIC. Please refer to the ACIC’s Character Standards for further information. 
    jobCategory Intelligence
    jobContact Peter
    jobContactPhone 0427 985 112
    jobNotes

    The ACIC is committed to diversity and inclusion. We celebrate and champion diversity to ensure we reflect the community we serve, and pride ourselves on our vibrant culture. We welcome applications from Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples, women, people with disability, people from culturally and linguistically diverse backgrounds, those who identify as LGBTIQ+, neurodiverse, mature aged employees and carers.

    This role is responsible for leading the design, development, and delivery of scalable, secure, and high-performing data solutions that support key organisational initiatives and strategic outcomes. Operating within a modern, cloud-enabled data environment, the position provides technical leadership across data engineering practices, ensuring alignment with enterprise data strategy, architecture standards, and governance requirements.


    The role drives the implementation and optimisation of data pipelines and integration processes (batch and real-time), leveraging the organisation’s enterprise data platform (including Cloudera) to enable reliable data ingestion, storage, and processing. It plays a critical part in shaping platform capability by evaluating emerging technologies and continuously enhancing engineering standards, patterns, and delivery practices.


    Working collaboratively with business stakeholders, architects, and cross-functional teams, the position translates complex business requirements into fit-for-purpose data solutions and supports end-to-end delivery of data products. The role also contributes to DevSecOps practices, automation, and observability, ensuring solutions are robust, production-ready, and aligned with compliance, security, and governance frameworks.


    In line with APS Executive Level 1 expectations, the position requires strong leadership, sound judgement, and the ability to manage competing priorities in a fast-paced environment. It also focuses on building organisational capability through mentoring, fostering continuous improvement, and maintaining strong stakeholder relationships to ensure data platforms meet current and future needs.

    • Lead the design and delivery of data solutions to support key delivery initiatives, ensuring alignment with enterprise data strategy, enterprise architecture standards, and organisational priorities.

    • Design, implement and maintain data solutions leveraging the Bureau’s Cloudera-based enterprise data platform, ensuring scalable, secure and governed data ingestion, storage and processing; and provide expert advice on, and evaluate, emerging technologies and tooling to ensure data platforms remain fit-for-purpose and aligned to future organisational needs. 
    • Optimise data processing workflows to improve performance, cost efficiency, scalability, and reliability across cloud-based and hybrid data platforms. 
    • Provide technical leadership in data engineering practices, establishing and embedding standards, patterns, and best practices for data integration, data modelling, and pipeline development. 
    • Collaborate closely with business stakeholders, product owners, and enterprise and solution architects to translate business requirements into fit-for-purpose data solutions, ensuring alignment with enterprise architecture and long-term platform direction. 
    • Engage with cross-functional teams (including data scientists, analysts, application teams, and platform engineers) to support end-to-end delivery of data products and services. 
    • Support delivery lifecycle activities, including planning, estimation, implementation, testing, deployment, and transition to operations, ensuring solutions are production-ready and supportable. 
    • Provide expert advice and recommendations on data platform capabilities, emerging technologies, and toolsets to continuously enhance the organisation’s data engineering capability and platform maturity. 
    • Mentor and guide junior engineers and team members, fostering capability uplift in modern data engineering techniques, tools, and practices. 
    • Ensure data solutions align with governance, security and compliance requirements, including data quality, metadata management, lineage, and auditing standards.

    departmentDescription At Defence Housing Australia, we provide quality housing and related services to Defence members and families. In doing this, we support the operational, recruitment and retention goals of the Department of Defence. To meet our Defence housing obligations, we are active in Australian residential housing markets, acquiring and developing land, and constructing and purchasing houses.     jobDescription

    Lead ESG & sustainability at DHA. Drive strategic change and shape a more sustainable future supporting Defence members and their families. Apply now!


    About us

    DHA was established in 1988 following passage of the Defence Housing Australia Act 1987 (DHA Act) to provide adequate and suitable housing for, and housing related services to, members of the Australian Defence Force (ADF) and their families. We remain committed to our purpose of providing housing, and housing related services, to ADF members and their families to support Defence’s operational needs.  


    DHA manages an expanding portfolio of housing solutions and provides housing for over 17,200 ADF members and their families and administers rent allowance for more than 16,000 ADF members and their families in private rental accommodation. DHA provides housing related services advising on eligibility, providing posting support, and managing bookings and allocation services for all DHA provisioned housing and on-base accommodation to approximately 60,000 ADF members. 


    To find out more about DHA, please refer to our website or read our latest Annual Report.
    jobDuties

    About your team

    The Governance and Communication Branch:

    • instils and continually reinforces a corporate culture of acting lawfully, ethically and responsibly in accordance with DHA’s legislative requirements and overarching governance framework
    • fosters relationships with and delivers quality service to DHA customers and stakeholders
    • guides and influences risk-based decision-making throughout the organisation.


    The work environment is dynamic, with a number of projects and initiatives being undertaken concurrently to improve the capability and performance of the organisation.


    About the role

    The Director, ESG and Sustainability provides enterprise leadership for the implementation, coordination and continuous improvement of DHA’s Environmental, Social and Governance (ESG) Strategy and Framework. Working under broad direction, the role translates DHA’s strategic ESG objectives into enterprise implementation priorities, governance arrangements, reporting and capability uplift, and provides authoritative advice on complex and emerging ESG matters. 


    The role integrates ESG considerations into business planning, risk management, performance reporting and organisational decision-making. It leads sustainability and climate-related reporting, strengthens ESG data governance, reporting capability and assurance arrangements, anticipates legislative and industry developments, and works through business owners to deliver shared enterprise outcomes. The role exercises a high degree of independence and judgement, manages ambiguity and competing priorities, and leads change to improve organisational ESG maturity.

    jobDescription

    The Ministerial, Parliamentary and Cabinet (MPC) team is responsible for delivering high-quality, timely parliamentary services and coordinating ministerial products for the department and Ministers' offices. The team provides ongoing support, training and centralised administration of the Parliamentary Document Management System (PDMS).


    As the Assistant Director, Parliamentary Workflow at the Department of Education you will lead the department's parliamentary workflow function and is responsible for ensuring the effective administration, governance and continuous improvement of the Parliamentary Document Management System (PDMS). The role oversees the delivery of accurate, timely and high-quality parliamentary services, supporting the department and Ministers' offices to meet parliamentary and ministerial requirements.


    As the Assistant Director, Ministerial Office Support, Parliamentary Committee Enquiries and Tabling at the Department of Education you will lead the department's engagement with Ministers' offices and coordinates parliamentary committee enquiries, tabling requirements and related parliamentary business. The role is responsible for overseeing the day-to-day management of parliamentary matters across the department, ensuring activities are delivered accurately, efficiently and within often competing and time-critical deadlines.

    jobDuties

    Assistant Director, Parliamentary Workflow 

    In this role, you will be responsible for the following:

    • Lead and develop strategic parliamentary services, ensuring adherence to best practice standards and compliance with government and department requirements, to deliver agency priorities
    • Foster an inclusive and positive team culture, provide leadership, and support team members to enhance their capability, professional development and contribute to a high performing team
    • Provide expert advice and guidance, quality assurance, identify innovative solutions, oversee compliance, share knowledge, and identify emerging issues
    • Collaborate with senior leaders and key stakeholders to drive parliamentary initiatives, and enhance policy and program outcomes to meet strategic objectives
    • Support the strategic direction of the department and manage and maintain Parliamentary Power BI reporting accurately, to enhance data-driven decision making
    • Manage the administration of the PDMS, including maintenance, enhancement and internal training and support
    • Support a continuous improvement approach and implement parliamentary processes and frameworks that support the team’s ongoing strategic and operational delivery of parliamentary services.


    Assistant Director, Ministerial Office Support, Parliamentary Committee Enquiries and Tabling

    • Foster an inclusive and positive team culture, provide leadership, and support team members to enhance their capability, professional development and contribute to a high performing team

    departmentDescription IP Australia is the Australian Government agency that drives innovation by administering intellectual property (IP) rights, including patents, trade marks, designs, and plant breeder’s rights. Our mission is simple: to help Australians turn great ideas into reality through a world-class IP system that fuels creativity, investment, and economic growth. Working with us means being part of a team that influences global IP frameworks, supports trade agreements, and educates businesses and individuals on the value of IP. We’re not just about protecting ideas—we’re about enabling innovation that benefits everyone. At IP Australia, we foster an inclusive and diverse culture where every individual feels respected and valued. We believe that diversity drives innovation, and we are committed to creating a workplace where everyone can thrive and reach their full potential. We understand the importance of flexibility and wellbeing. That’s why we offer hybrid work arrangements, flexible hours, and programs to support mental health and overall wellbeing. Our focus on psychological safety ensures that employees feel secure and supported in their roles. Professional growth is a priority for us. We provide opportunities for skill development, education support, and clear pathways for career progression. Whether you’re starting your career or looking to advance, IP Australia offers the tools and resources to help you succeed. Our benefits are designed to support your lifestyle and future. With competitive conditions such as 15.4% superannuation contributions, salary packaging options, and family-friendly policies, we make sure our employees are well taken care of. Finally, integrity is at the heart of everything we do. We uphold the highest standards of fairness and respect, maintaining a workplace built on trust and collaboration. At IP Australia, you’ll be part of an organisation that values both people and purpose.
    jobDescription

    We are seeking Assistant Directors to help improve Plant Breeder’s Rights services, staff and customer experience, and business systems. ​

    ​

    Working within a small multidisciplinary team, you will engage with staff and customers, decide what improvements matter most, turn needs into project plans, delivery plans and requirements, and guide changes to PBR systems, services, practices, and processes through implementation and adoption.​

    ​

    Responsibilities will be allocated according to skills, experience, capacity and business priorities. You may have a stronger focus on product ownership or project delivery, while working collaboratively across both areas and providing cover when needed to maintain essential services.​ While the role will not initially have any direct reports, it is expected to recruit and supervise a new change and communications role anticipated to be established soon after appointment.

    ​

    The work includes:

    • Working with customers, staff and subject matter experts to understand needs and identify opportunities to improve systems, services, practices, and processes.
    • Prioritising, scoping, planning, and delivering projects and system improvements by balancing customer value, organisational priorities, legislative obligations, risks and available resources.
    • Gathering and documenting detailed business and user requirements, expected outcomes and acceptance criteria.
    • Planning and coordinating transition and adoption activities to prepare customers and staff for change and support successful implementation.
    • Monitoring and reporting on progress, risks, issues and benefits, and collaborating with Product Owners and delivery teams to support consistent experiences across IP Australia’s business systems.
    jobDuties

    The successful candidates will demonstrate capabilities in:


    • Planning and managing projects: defining scope, outcomes and responsibilities; preparing fit-for-purpose project and delivery plans; and monitoring progress, risks and dependencies.

    • Planning and supporting change: helping users understand and prepare for change, supporting adoption, and measuring uptake to identify where further support is needed.

    • Understanding needs in a regulatory environment: identifying customer and business needs and balancing them with organisational priorities, legislative obligations, risks, effort and available resources.

    • Defining system requirements: working with customers, staff and subject matter experts to translate needs into clear, testable requirements, acceptance criteria and expected outcomes.

    • Analysing information and solving problems: applying evidence, judgement and practical analysis to inform decisions, resolve barriers and support delivery.

    • Communicating and collaborating: engaging clearly with a variety of stakeholders orally and in writing to understand needs and support change management.

    • Taking ownership and delivering outcomes: working autonomously, meeting commitments and delivering work to the required standard and timeframe.

    • Supervising and developing an employee: setting clear expectations, providing feedback, managing performance and supporting capability development.


    You do not need to have held a Product Owner or Project Manager title. We welcome relevant experience across product, project and business improvement work.
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    Under section 22(8) of the Public Service Act 1999, employees must be Australian citizens to be employed in the Australian Public Service (APS).

    Some applicants may be able to obtain and maintain a security clearance at a specified clearance level – please check the vacancy information section for security clearance requirements.

    jobDescription

    The Organisation

    The Australian Security Intelligence Organisation (ASIO) protects Australia and Australians from threats to their security. ASIO’s people are ordinary Australians but they do extraordinary things – they are our most important asset. To be successful, we need talented people who are highly capable, dedicated, adaptable and resilient.


    We seek to reflect the community we protect. ASIO is committed to fostering a diverse and inclusive environment, where all staff are valued and respected. We welcome applications from all eligible candidates, irrespective of gender, sexual orientation, ethnicity, religious affiliation, age or disability. We are culturally and linguistically diverse, language skills enhance our collective ability to protect Australia. Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples are encouraged to apply. We are secretive about what we do, not what we value.


    At a time when Australia’s security environment is dynamic, diverse and degrading, our work is more important than ever. Join the mission and make a difference every day. 


    The Opportunity


    ASIO is seeking a technical lead software engineer/exploitation specialist to develop, integrate and maintain capabilities in support of ASIO’s warranted computer access requirements. You will research and develop new and novel approaches to meet a variety of technical intelligence collection challenges and respond to the rapid pace of technology development. In this role, you will work in a broad technical collection group where teamwork and effective communication is imperative. 

    You will work closely with internal stakeholders and industry partners to provide integrated technical solutions to enable some of our most complex collection tasks. In partnership with operational teams, you will enable the deployment of these capabilities to directly support ASIO’s mission.

    A merit pool may be created to fill future vacancies which have the same or similar requirements to this position. This merit pool will be valid for up to 18 months.
    jobDuties

    Responsibilities

     

    As a Technical Lead – Software Engineer/Exploitation Specialist in ASIO, you will:


    • Translate high-level organisational intelligence requirements into a sustainable technical strategy. Anticipate shifts in the global digital landscape to ensure the agency maintains a competitive technical advantage. 
    • Provide leadership and apply your own subject matter expertise to set technical standards and processes.
    • Deliver sensitive capabilities to enable computer access collection.
    • Build relationships with industry, domestic and international intelligence partners to develop complex capabilities.
    • Effectively communicate strategy, risks and opportunities to a range of technical and non-technical audiences in order to secure investment and cooperation.
    • Support sensitive operations to enable intelligence collection requirements against ASIO priorities.
    • Mentor and develop technical staff to build strategic capacity for ASIO to enhance its capabilities into the future.
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    What you will bring

    We are seeking candidates with the following capabilities:


    • Demonstrated ability to shape the strategic direction of development activities to ensure alignment with overarching organisational need and evolving changes in the technology landscape.
    • Commitment to technical excellence and rigor, with a demonstrated ability to identify risk and implement quality assurance frameworks for the testing, validation, and deployment of tools.
    • Proven ability to cultivate and leverage strategic partnerships with external vendors, and inter-agency partners to enhance capability.
    • Proven track record of mentoring highly specialised technical staff, fostering a culture of continuous learning, and translating complex technical achievements into value-statements for non-technical stakeholders.
    • Proven ability to balance competing priorities and exercise sound judgement.


    You are not required to address each capability separately in your application. Consider providing examples that illustrate multiple capabilities at once.


    Refer to ASIO’s Core Capability Framework to understand what is expected at each level for each capability.


    Skills and qualifications 


    Candidates are required to have one of the following tertiary qualifications or relevant industry qualifications in: 


    • Information Technology, Computer Science or Cyber Security
    • Network, Electrical, Telecommunications, Computer or Mechanical Engineering
    • Other relevant Science, Technical, Engineering and Mathematical fields not covered above. 


    Applicants must also be able to demonstrate one or more of the following:


    • Prior experience in ethical hacking, vulnerability research and/or penetration testing.
    • Experience in digital forensics - computer, mobile, network and/or malware.
    • Strong understanding of cyber and information security concepts. 
    • Software and hardware development, testing, integration and/or deployment including experience in Continuous Integration/Continuous Development and DevOps concepts.
    • Understanding of mobile and personal computing operating systems.
    • Understanding of networking protocols.
    • Experience with reverse engineering.
    • An understanding of how AI technology can be used to augment technical research and development tasks, including creating custom harnesses.

    Eligibility

     

    To be eligible to work at ASIO, you must be: 

    • an Australian citizen
    • assessed as suitable to hold and maintain a TOP SECRET-Privileged Access (TS-PA) security clearance.

    Reasonable adjustments


    Please let us know if you require any additional assistance or reasonable adjustments during any stage of the recruitment process in order to fully participate in the recruitment process or the workplace. This may include reasonable adjustments to assessment methodologies to enable full participation.

    What we offer you 


    ASIO provides a number of benefits to its staff including:


    • A competitive salary, including a 7.5% allowance for maintaining a TOP SECRET-Privileged Access (TS-PA) security clearance.
    • Employer superannuation contributions of 15.4%.
    • A variety of leave options in addition to the standard 4 weeks annual leave, including stand down between Christmas and New Year.
    • Flexible working arrangements. Please note due to our unique working environment, work from home options are not available. 
    • Study assistance, including financial support and study leave for tertiary education.
    • Access to 7 staff-led diversity and inclusion networks.
    • Access to an Employee Assistance Program (EAP).

    Location


    Canberra, ACT. Other locations may be considered according to the needs of the Organisation.

    Relocation assistance is available if required.

    We advertise positions nationally to reach the broadest audience. Please refer to the above location before applying.


    How to apply


    ASIO officers’ identities are protected by law. Under section 92 of the ASIO Act, the only declared ASIO officers are the Director-General and Deputy Directors-General. Due to the need to protect our people’s identities, ASIO holds all employment applications in the strictest of confidence. It is essential that you do the same. Please do not discuss your application with others as doing so may adversely affect your potential employment.


    Click on ‘Apply online’ to commence your application. Your application must be complete and include the following:

    • A written pitch of up to 800 words that addresses the required capabilities for the role. Use examples to demonstrate how your skills and experience meet the capabilities. 
    • A current CV, no more than 2 pages in length, outlining your employment history, the dates and a brief description of your role, as well as any academic qualifications or relevant training you have undertaken.
    • Details of 2 referees, which must include a current or former supervisor. ASIO will not contact your referees without advising you prior to doing so. 

    Before applying read the ASIO People Capability Framework and Core Capability Guide, both available on the ASIO website. To increase your chances of success, tailor your application responses to align with the relevant capabilities and levels outlined in these frameworks.


    Use of artificial intelligence in recruitment processes


    ASIO does not use any form of artificial intelligence (AI) at any point in the recruitment process.

    All applications are reviewed by a selection panel. We recognise some applicants may use AI technologies to draft applications or prepare for interviews. If you wish to use AI, we recommend doing this only for initial drafts and ensure that your application is personalised and accurately reflects your skills, experience and qualifications.


    Additionally, be aware of the privacy policies of any AI tool you use. Be mindful of the information you are entering into AI tools (personal details, work history, etc) and how using it may link you to potential ASIO employment, noting that you are expected to be discrete and maintain confidentiality with your application.


    If you are invited to attend interview, the use of AI tools and recording devices during interview is strictly prohibited. Should AI tools or recording services be detected, your application, and any future applications may be discontinued.


    The recruitment selection process  


    Our selection process is rigorous and extensive. All employment decisions and selection processes at ASIO are based on merit principles and candidates must be prepared to undergo various selection stages. The stages may include application assessment, eligibility checks, online testing, skills-based assessment and interview. 


    We ask all applicants for their patience throughout the process. 


    Employment conditions


    Employment is under the Australian Security Intelligence Organisation Act 1979. Conditions of service are similar to those applying in the Australian Public Service. We recognise entitlements accrued under the Public Service Act 1999 and provide for continuation of superannuation under the Commonwealth schemes. Salary packaging arrangements are also available.


    Australian Workplace Equality Index


    ASIO has achieved the Australian Workplace Equality Index (AWEI) Gold Standard in 2024, 2025 and 2026, recognising ASIO’s inclusion work and positive culture. ASIO was the first Australian intelligence agency to achieve this status.


    For more information about ASIO, please visit: www.asio.gov.au.
