    The Organisation


    The Australian Security Intelligence Organisation (ASIO) protects Australia and Australians from threats to their security. ASIO’s people are ordinary Australians but they do extraordinary things – they are our most important asset. To be successful, we need talented people who are highly capable, dedicated, adaptable and resilient.


    We seek to reflect the community we protect. ASIO is committed to fostering a diverse and inclusive environment, where all staff are valued and respected. We welcome applications from all eligible candidates, irrespective of gender, sexual orientation, ethnicity, religious affiliation, age or disability. We are culturally and linguistically diverse, language skills enhance our collective ability to protect Australia. Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples are encouraged to apply. We are secretive about what we do, not what we value.


    At a time when Australia’s security environment is dynamic, diverse and degrading, our work is more important than ever. Join the mission and make a difference every day. 


    The Opportunity

    ASIO builds novel technical solutions to solve complex technical challenges. Our solutions ensure we have the platforms, tools and capabilities available to keep Australia safe. We are seeking talented technologists with experience in hardware and software reverse engineering to build our next generation of capability.

    This work is dynamic, with staff working across multiple hardware and software platforms and frameworks.


    In this role, you will need to be able to solve problems while working in a multi-disciplined team environment. You will work closely with partners and commercial providers to ensure a shared understanding of capability through a continuous feedback cycle.

    ASIO looks for candidates who are curious, resilient and enjoy problem solving. To be successful in this role, you will demonstrate curiosity and innovation to build novel solutions, be willing to continuously grow, and refine your skills to keep up with the pace of technical change.


    A merit pool may be created to fill future vacancies which have the same or similar requirements to this position. This merit pool will be valid for up to 18 months.
    jobDuties

    Responsibilities


    As a Reverse Engineer in ASIO, you will:


    • Undertake holistic assessments and teardowns of hardware and associated software.
    • Assist in delivery of projects in conjunction with partners and commercial providers.
    • Integrate capabilities into standard frameworks.
    • Assist in building and maintaining highly effective relationships with partners and commercial providers.
    • Work in a team environment ensuring a shared understanding of capability and current work status with co-workers.


    As a Senior Reverse Engineer at the SITEC level, in addition to the above you will: 

    • Lead the development and application of novel reverse engineering techniques to inform vulnerability research approaches. This includes opportunities to integrate approved and ethical use of Artificial Intelligence (AI).
    • Lead selected strategic industry outreach programs ensuring a shared understanding of ASIO’s needs.
    • Develop and upskill junior team members.
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    What you will bring


    We are seeking candidates with one or more of the following capabilities: 

    • Hardware reverse engineering skills and/or experience in developing embedded devices.
    • Software reverse engineering skills, including both static and dynamic analysis.
    • Experience in vulnerability research, penetration testing and digital forensics.
    • Experience in network protocol analysis particularly at the application layer as well as identifying custom protocols. Knowledge of RF including Wi-Fi and Bluetooth as well as other related ISM protocols.
    • Knowledge of operating system internals (Linux, iOS, Android and RTOS).
    • Experience in software development including the ability to interpret software code and improve processes and capabilities.
    • Knowledge of how AI technology can be used to augment reverse engineering work and the expertise to develop and utilise custom harnesses to enable this.
    • Effective communication skills including the ability to present technical information in a clear, concise and articulate manner.
    • Demonstrated experience in building and maintaining effective working relationships with key stakeholders.

    In addition to the above, as a Senior Reverse Engineer at the SITE C level, you will bring the following capabilities: 

    • Experience in training and developing junior staff.
    • Demonstrated ability to engage with partners and support industry outreach programs.
    • Ability to undertake project reviews to identify improvements and shape the strategic direction of future projects.

    You are not required to address each capability separately in your application. Consider providing examples that illustrate multiple capabilities at once.


    Refer to ASIO’s Core Capability Framework to understand what is expected at each level for each capability.


    Skills and qualifications 


    Candidates are required to have the following qualifications: 

    • Relevant tertiary qualification such as Software/Electronics Engineering, Computer Science, Information Technology, Cyber Security or similar.
    • In lieu of a formal tertiary qualification, be able to demonstrate relevant experience within a technical specialist role: A minimum of 1-3 years’ experience for the ITE2 role and 4-6 years for the SITEC role.
    • The following qualifications are desirable, but are not required: 
    • Relevant industry qualifications such as GIAC, OSCP or similar.
    • 1-3 relevant years of experience within intelligence, military or law enforcement.

    Eligibility


    To be eligible to work at ASIO, you must be: 

    • an Australian citizen
    • assessed as suitable to hold and maintain a TOP SECRET-Privileged Access (TS-PA) security clearance.

    Reasonable adjustments


    Please let us know if you require any additional assistance or reasonable adjustments during any stage of the recruitment process in order to fully participate in the recruitment process or the workplace. This may include reasonable adjustments to assessment methodologies to enable full participation.

    What we offer you

     

    ASIO provides a number of benefits to its staff including:

    • A competitive salary, including a 7.5% allowance for maintaining a TOP SECRET-Privileged Access (TS-PA) security clearance.
    • Employer superannuation contributions of 15.4%.
    • A variety of leave options in addition to the standard 4 weeks annual leave, including stand down between Christmas and New Year.
    • Flexible working arrangements. Please note due to our unique working environment, work from home options are not available. 
    • Study assistance, including financial support and study leave for tertiary education.
    • Access to 7 staff-led diversity and inclusion networks.
    • Access to an Employee Assistance Program (EAP).

    Location


    Canberra, ACT. Other locations may be considered according to the needs of the Organisation. 

    Relocation assistance is available if required.


    We advertise positions nationally to reach the broadest audience. Please refer to the above location before applying.


    How to apply


    ASIO officers’ identities are protected by law. Under section 92 of the ASIO Act, the only declared ASIO officers are the Director-General and Deputy Directors-General. Due to the need to protect our people’s identities, ASIO holds all employment applications in the strictest of confidence. It is essential that you do the same. Please do not discuss your application with others as doing so may adversely affect your potential employment.


    Click on ‘Apply online’ to commence your application. Your application must be complete and include the following:

    • A written pitch of up to 800 words that addresses the required capabilities for the role. Use examples to demonstrate how your skills and experience meet the capabilities. 
    • A current CV, no more than 2 pages in length, outlining your employment history, the dates and a brief description of your role, as well as any academic qualifications or relevant training you have undertaken.
    • Details of 2 referees, which must include a current or former supervisor. ASIO will not contact your referees without advising you prior to doing so. 

    Before applying read the ASIO People Capability Framework and Core Capability Guide, both available on the ASIO website. To increase your chances of success, tailor your application responses to align with the relevant capabilities and levels outlined in these frameworks.


    Use of artificial intelligence in recruitment processes


    ASIO does not use any form of artificial intelligence (AI) at any point in the recruitment process.

    All applications are reviewed by a selection panel. We recognise some applicants may use AI technologies to draft applications or prepare for interviews. If you wish to use AI, we recommend doing this only for initial drafts and ensure that your application is personalised and accurately reflects your skills, experience and qualifications.


    Additionally, be aware of the privacy policies of any AI tool you use. Be mindful of the information you are entering into AI tools (personal details, work history, etc) and how using it may link you to potential ASIO employment, noting that you are expected to be discrete and maintain confidentiality with your application.


    If you are invited to attend interview, the use of AI tools and recording devices during interview is strictly prohibited. Should AI tools or recording services be detected, your application, and any future applications may be discontinued.


    The recruitment selection process  


    Our selection process is rigorous and extensive. All employment decisions and selection processes at ASIO are based on merit principles and candidates must be prepared to undergo various selection stages. The stages may include application assessment, eligibility checks, online testing, skills-based assessment and interview. 


    We ask all applicants for their patience throughout the process. 


    Australian Workplace Equality Index


    Employment is under the Australian Security Intelligence Organisation Act 1979. Conditions of service are similar to those applying in the Australian Public Service. We recognise entitlements accrued under the Public Service Act 1999 and provide for continuation of superannuation under the Commonwealth schemes. Salary packaging arrangements are also available.


    Australian Workplace Equality Index


    ASIO has achieved the Australian Workplace Equality Index (AWEI) Gold Standard in 2024, 2025 and 2026, recognising ASIO’s inclusion work and positive culture. ASIO was the first Australian intelligence agency to achieve this status.


    For more information about ASIO, please visit: www.asio.gov.au.

    The Organisation


    The Australian Security Intelligence Organisation (ASIO) protects Australia and Australians from threats to their security. ASIO’s people are ordinary Australians but they do extraordinary things – they are our most important asset. To be successful, we need talented people who are highly capable, dedicated, adaptable and resilient.

    We seek to reflect the community we protect. ASIO is committed to fostering a diverse and inclusive environment, where all staff are valued and respected. We welcome applications from all eligible candidates, irrespective of gender, sexual orientation, ethnicity, religious affiliation, age or disability. We are culturally and linguistically diverse, language skills enhance our collective ability to protect Australia. Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples are encouraged to apply. We are secretive about what we do, not what we value.

    At a time when Australia’s security environment is dynamic, diverse and degrading, our work is more important than ever. Join the mission and make a difference every day.


    The Opportunity


    ASIO is striving to build the future of operational technology. We need forward-thinking leaders who enjoy turning complex challenges into breakthrough solutions. As an Assistant Director leading one of our operational capability development teams, you will play a vital role in building capability and shaping the tools that keep Australians safe.

    You will head a talented group of engineers and technologists, guiding them from concept through to long-term sustainment of operational technology. Your day-to-day responsibilities will blend strategic planning, hands-on problem solving and stakeholder engagement. You will ensure solutions meet ASIO’s security standards, manage procurements, and assist with keeping budgets on track—all while keeping your team ahead of emerging-technology trends.

    In this role, you will maintain strong, trusted relationships with external suppliers, other government agencies and international partners. You will work with senior managers and executives to enable early risk identification and transparent reporting.

    If you thrive leading technical teams, enjoy being part of a team solving tough problems and want a role where your work makes a real difference, this is the opportunity for you.

    ASIO looks for candidates who are curious, resilient and enjoy problem solving. To be successful in this role, you will build effective and enduring partnerships and enjoy leading highly technical teams while remaining focused on longer-term objectives.

    A merit pool may be created to fill future vacancies which have the same or similar requirements to this position. This merit pool will be valid for up to 18 months.

    • Contribute to and assist with the management of directorate budgets.

    The Organisation


    The Australian Security Intelligence Organisation (ASIO) protects Australia and Australians from threats to their security. ASIO’s people are ordinary Australians but they do extraordinary things – they are our most important asset. To be successful, we need talented people who are highly capable, dedicated, adaptable and resilient.

    The Opportunity


    ASIO is seeking software engineers and exploitation specialists to develop, integrate and maintain capabilities in support of ASIO’s warranted computer access requirements. You will research and develop new and novel approaches to meet a variety of technical intelligence collection challenges and respond to the rapid pace of technology development. In this role, you will work in a broad technical collection group where teamwork and effective communication is imperative.

    You will work closely with internal stakeholders and industry partners to provide integrated technical solutions to enable some of our most complex collection tasks. In partnership with operational teams, you will enable the deployment of these capabilities to directly support ASIO’s mission.

    A merit pool may be created to fill future vacancies which have the same or similar requirements to this position. This merit pool will be valid for up to 18 months.
    jobDuties

    Responsibilities

    As a Software Engineer/Exploitation Specialist at ITE1.1/ITE2 level, you will:


    • Maintain awareness of mobile, desktop and network technologies to identify emerging threats and opportunities that impact access and collection capabilities.
    • Collaborate with industry and intelligence partners to develop technical solutions and transition exploitation capabilities to operations.
    • Conduct capability testing and work with other internal stakeholders to coordinate end-to-end operational testing of system components.
    • Provide training and support to operational teams to enable the deployment of exploitation capabilities, including during ASIO operational activities. This may require activity outside standard working hours.

    In addition to the above, as a Senior Exploitation Specialist at the SITE C level, you will:

    ﻿

    • Provide mentoring and support to junior staff.
    • Leverage highly developed interpersonal, oral and written communication skills.
    • Develop and/or integrate software and tooling, spanning more than one language and domain.
    • Lead and manage high level system design within teams across Computer Access capabilities and projects to ensure interoperability, scalability and sustainability.
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    What you will bring

    We are seeking candidates with the following capabilities:


    ITE1.1/ITE2 (APS5/APS6) level:


    • Adheres strictly to security protocols while proactively identifying potential risks within the collection pipeline to protect the Organisation and its data sources.
    • Applies technical problem-solving to resolve immediate failures and designs technical workflows that anticipate future operational needs and technological shifts.
    • Works collaboratively with peers and stakeholders, delivers high-quality technical outputs and coordinates the delivery and sustainment of bespoke toolsets from inception to operational use.
    • Contributes to the long-term roadmap for digital collection capabilities, balancing internal development with industry partnerships.
    • Builds and sustains strategic partnerships across government, industry, and international agencies.
    • Clearly explains technical issues and translates complex constraints into actionable options for operational stakeholders.
    • Ability to develop, support, and sustain complex systems, with a strong background in one or more of the following:
    • ﻿Full stack development, Linux/ARM, system administration, mobile and digital application development, vulnerability research or digital forensics


    SITE C (EL1) level:


    • Oversees the security posture of the technical estate, ensuring that all bespoke tool development and online collection activity is risk-managed and compliant with overarching security directives.
    • Translates high-level organisational intelligence requirements into a sustainable technical strategy. Anticipates shifts in the global digital landscape to ensure the agency maintains a competitive technical advantage.
    • Manages the end-to-end lifecycle of technical capabilities, ensuring that resources are allocated efficiently to deliver high-impact tools on time and within scope.
    • Leads multidisciplinary teams and fosters strategic alliances with key industry leaders and government partners to accelerate capability acquisition.
    • Demonstrates exceptional leadership and accountability, upholding the highest standards of professional conduct and safeguarding sensitive technical capabilities.
    • Acts as a primary technical advisor to senior executives, articulating complex technical risks and opportunities to drive strategic investment and operational change.
    • Ability to develop, support, and sustain complex systems, with a strong background in one or more of the following:
    • Full stack development, Linux/ARM, system administration, mobile and digital application development, vulnerability research or digital forensics
    • Possesses the depth of knowledge to challenge technical assumptions and validate the viability of complex digital collection solutions.

    You are not required to address each capability separately in your application. Consider providing examples that illustrate multiple capabilities at once.


    Refer to ASIO’s Core Capability Framework to understand what is expected at each level for each capability.


    Skills and qualifications

    Candidates are required to have one of the following tertiary qualifications or relevant industry qualifications in:

    • Information Technology, Computer Science or Cyber Security
    • Network, Electrical, Telecommunications, Computer or Mechanical Engineering
    • Other relevant Science, Technical, Engineering and Mathematical fields not covered above.


    The following interests and experience in one or more of the following is desirable, but not required:

    • Full stack software development / Linux / ARM development
    • System and network administration
    • Mobile file systems, forensics and data collection
    • Ethical hacking, vulnerability research and/or penetration testing
    • Telecommunications systems, cellular (Core and protocol experience) and non-cellular (Wi-Fi, Bluetooth and related ISM) networks
    • Full stack software development and hardware development, testing, integration and/or deployment including experience in Continuous Integration/Continuous Development and DevOps concepts
    • Reverse Engineering (Software and/or Hardware).

    To be eligible to work at ASIO, you must be:

    • an Australian citizen
    • assessed as suitable to hold and maintain a TOP SECRET-Privileged Access (TS-PA) security clearance.

    departmentDescription The Clean Energy Regulator is the Government body responsible for administering legislation to reduce carbon emissions and increase the use of clean energy. The Clean Energy Regulator has administrative responsibilities for the: • National Greenhouse and Energy Reporting Scheme, under the National Greenhouse and Energy Reporting Act 2007 • Emissions Reduction Fund, under the Carbon Credits (Carbon Farming Initiative) Act 2011, • Renewable Energy Target, under the Renewable Energy (Electricity) Act 2000, and • Australian National Registry of Emissions Units, under the Australian National Registry of Emissions Units Act 2011, • Nature Repair Market, under the Nature Repair Act 2023, • Guarantee of Origin, under the Future Made in Australia (Guarantee of Origin) Bill 2024. Our purpose is to accelerate carbon abatement for Australia. We work with our stakeholders and clients including Australian Government departments and agencies, industry bodies, liable entities and the community to provide regulatory services of the highest standard. We offer challenging and rewarding work in administering legislation that will reduce carbon emissions and increase the use of clean energy. Our agency is committed to workplace diversity and aims to create an environment that values and utilises the contribution of people from different backgrounds, experiences and perspectives. We encourage applications from Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander People, people with a disability, people from diverse cultural and linguistic backgrounds and mature age workers
    jobDescription

    About the Branch


    The Transformation Branch works collaboratively to turn strategy into measurable value for the agency. By bringing together future strategy, business and data architecture, data management and analytics, and strong governance, we support the delivery of agency priorities in a consistent, transparent and sustainable way. The Branch plays a central role in aligning strategy, investment and delivery, ensuring initiatives are prioritised, integrated and informed by evidence.


    The following Sections are part of the Branch:


    Value Management Office (VMO)


    Provides agency-wide value management, governance and portfolio oversight to support informed investment decisions and maximise value delivered through the agency’s transformation portfolio. The team leads governance, reporting, risk management, benefits realisation and portfolio planning to support informed decision-making and accountability. Using SAFe Agile and portfolio management practices, it guides initiatives from onboarding through delivery and transition to business-as-usual, while embedding consistent governance across the Change Program.


    Strategy and Business Architecture


    Supports business lines in understanding their current and future states, and


    • identifies opportunities for improvement in efficiency and effectiveness
    • owns and maintains the repository of business capabilities, ensuring these are linked to people (org structures and accountability), strategic themes, processes, information assets, and technology
    • owns and maintains the repository of data entities, repositories, data owners, and data architecture, increasing alignment of terminology, understanding, and data captured at source, and
    • enables evidence-based decision-making by linking business capabilities and data to current and future requirements, existing investments, and identifying gaps for required investment.


    Key functions include the ownership of Business Capability Mapping, Business Repositories, Strategic Alignment, Investment Linkages, Process Integration, Technology Alignment, and Data Architecture and Governance.


    Product Management


    Connects strategy, user needs, and delivery to ensure the agency builds the right thing at the right time. The function helps translate policy intent, scheme outcomes, and operational needs into a clear, prioritised pipeline of work that delivers measurable value. The team’s work supports the delivery of CER’s purpose by enabling effective administration of schemes and improving how participants, industry, and staff interact with our systems. They ensure that ideas and opportunities are turned into well-defined, evidence-based initiatives, and that delivery is aligned to strategic outcomes and measurable benefits. They are responsible for shaping and managing the agency’s product portfolio, including defining product vision, maintaining roadmaps, and prioritising initiatives, epics, and features. Product owners lead product development to build the thing right by developing solutions that are


    desirable for users, feasible to deliver, and viable within regulatory and operational constraints. Product Management plays a central role across the delivery pipeline, providing transparency of priorities, enabling informed decision-making, and ensuring delivery efforts are focused on the highest-value outcomes.


    Organisational Change Management and Support


    Provides a central point for organisational change, information governance, contracts, procurement and branch support, ensuring change is well planned, clearly communicated and successfully adopted. We turn strategic priorities, including the Change Program and digital delivery agenda, into practical actions that deliver real outcomes. The team provides trusted advice, simple frameworks and strong assurance to keep initiatives aligned with our operating model, regulatory requirements and corporate priorities. The team builds capability across the agency by embedding consistent change practices, lifting understanding of digital delivery, and improving how we communicate internally and externally. The team supports better decision-making through high-quality products, clear reporting and strong contract oversight. Through this work, we make it easier for the agency to deliver change, improving consistency, transparency and efficiency. This strengthens how we operate, supports reliable regulatory outcomes, maintains scheme integrity and builds confidence with stakeholders.


    Data Innovation and Tools (DI&T)


    Enables the agency to get the most value from its data by improving data quality, accessibility and usability. It develops tools, insights and data practices that support informed decision-making, increase efficiency and improve business outcomes. More details are available below.


    About the Section


    The Data Innovation & Tools Section enables the Clean Energy Regulator to operate as a data-driven regulator by delivering timely, trusted, reusable and scalable data capabilities that support scheme integrity, regulatory decision-making and market transparency. We lead the development and delivery of the agency’s enterprise data strategy, translating business priorities into governed data products, integrated platforms and actionable insights. This work strengthens data foundations across all schemes, ensuring data is consistent, high quality and accessible to internal and external stakeholders.


    The section advances Data Services to enable secure, efficient data sharing and interoperability across systems, participants and markets, while driving automation of high-value regulatory processes to reduce manual effort, improve timeliness and strengthen auditability. By embedding data governance, quality controls and standardised delivery patterns, we deliver reliable, scalable and fit-for-purpose solutions. We partner closely with business, policy and technology teams to co-design and deliver outcomes aligned to regulatory priorities. Through these efforts, the section strengthens the agency’s ability to manage risk, support informed decision-making and maintain confidence in administered schemes and markets, while positioning the organisation to meet future regulatory and data demands.


    About the Role


    As Manager, Data Innovation & Tools, you will lead a high-performing team, set priorities and oversee the delivery of the section’s work program.


    As part of the Transformation Branch, DI&T provides the information, data, and tools that support the agency’s Change Program and ongoing operations. Working closely with the Digital Services Branch and AI Business Unit, you will drive practical, sustainable solutions that meet evolving business needs, support regulatory decision-making, and deliver lasting organisational improvements.
    jobDuties

    As Manager, Data Innovation and Tools will be responsible for, but not limited to:


    • Leading a multidisciplinary team, setting clear expectations, supporting and developing staff, and taking responsibility for results.
    • Advising on information, data, and digital services needed by the Branch, the Change Program, and the agency.
    • Directing the work program, allocating resources, and managing risks and dependencies to meet agreed commitments.
    • Using the Transformation Office and Digital Services planning, delivery, governance, reporting, and assurance arrangements.
    • Building relationships across the agency and using architecture, business analysis, delivery, information governance, and security expertise across Transformation, Digital Services, and the AI Business Unit rather than duplicating those functions.
    • Representing DI&T in governance forums and cross-agency work, reporting progress, and raising risks and matters requiring a decision.
    • Re-engineering processes and developing information, data, and tools for use across the agency, while keeping agreed work on track and making resource and timing trade-offs clear.


    Knowledge, Skills and Attributes


    Applicants should demonstrate a record of:


    • Leading teams, delegating effectively, and developing staff.
    • Engaging stakeholders, negotiating competing interests, and influencing decisions across teams.
    • Explaining complex issues clearly and giving sound advice.
    • Exercising independent judgement and using evidence to make decisions within delegated authority.
    • Applying systems thinking and process re-engineering to understand dependencies and simplify work from start to finish.
    • Delivering agreed work despite uncertainty and competing demands.


    Experience leading complex delivery or improvement work is essential. You do not need to be a technical specialist, but you must be able to direct work across disciplines and test expert advice.

    jobDescription

    The APVMA is Australia's national regulator of agricultural and veterinary chemicals. Its work protects the health and safety of people, animals and the environment, and supports primary industries, biosecurity and international trade. 


    A Board-governed Commonwealth entity in the Agriculture, Fisheries and Forestry portfolio, the APVMA has its head office in Armidale NSW, and ~100 staff in Canberra ACT.


    Take ownership of the full CFO remit, with the opportunity to shape the financial future of a national regulator. With a new cost recovery framework in place and a finance modernisation program ahead, there's real scope to influence how the APVMA operates for years to come.


    For a Deputy CFO or Finance Director in a larger agency, this is a step up in scope rather than classification. You'll work directly with the CEO and Board, supported by a CPA-qualified Chief Operating Officer who is actively backing the finance modernisation program. You'll also benefit from a thorough handover ahead of the current CFO's retirement in 2027.


    • Shape long-term financial sustainability, developing the strategy for an agency that recovers almost 100% of its costs from industry
    • Lead finance modernisation, replacing manual processes with automation and making better use of data
    • Gain direct Board exposure, preparing and presenting financial advice - valuable experience for those with SES ambitions
    • Influence major investment decisions, contributing to a significant IT investment proposal involving Finance, DAFF and the DTA
    • Lead an experienced team, giving you the capacity to focus on strategy, leadership and change
    • Work for a bigger purpose, supporting a national regulator that protects people, animals and the environment.

    Reporting to the COO, you'll lead the APVMA's Finance and Procurement section, managing a team of eight across Armidale and Canberra. 


    Working closely with the executive leadership team, your responsibilities will include:

    • Develop and deliver the agency's financial strategy, including long-term sustainability, funding arrangements and resource planning
    • Provide strategic financial advice to the CEO, Board and senior executives, supporting key financial and investment decisions
    • Oversee financial planning, budgeting, forecasting, reporting and cost recovery arrangements, including CRIS implementation and pricing reviews
    • Lead financial and procurement governance, ensuring sound risk management and compliance with Commonwealth frameworks and legislation
    • Modernise finance systems and processes, including TechnologyOne, with a focus on automation, data and stronger financial controls
    • Build financial capability across the agency, helping leaders make informed financial and investment decisions
    • Lead and develop your team, while building strong relationships with central agencies, auditors and other key stakeholders.
    jobDuties

    Key responsibilities include:

    • Working with the CEO, COO and other senior executives to develop and implement the APVMA's strategic financial direction, including long-term financial sustainability strategies, resource management approaches and funding arrangements that support organisational priorities.
    • Providing high-quality strategic and financial advice to the APVMA Board, CEO, and senior executive on financial management, business sustainability, and investment decisions.
    • Leading enterprise-wide financial planning, budgeting, forecasting and financial reporting activities, ensuring the provision of accurate, timely and insightful information to support decision-making and accountability.
    • Leading the development, implementation, review and ongoing management of the APVMA’s cost recovery arrangements and Cost Recovery Implementation Statement processes, including pricing reviews, financial modelling, stakeholder engagement and compliance with the Australian Government Charging Framework.
    • Maintaining effective financial governance frameworks and ensuring compliance with the Public Governance, Performance and Accountability Act 2013 (PGPA Act), Australian Accounting Standards, the Commonwealth Resource Management Framework and other government policies and legislative requirements.
    • Providing strategic leadership of procurement governance and assurance arrangements to ensure value for money, effective risk management and compliance with Commonwealth procurement requirements.
    • Driving continuous improvement of financial, procurement and business systems, processes and controls to enhance efficiency, effectiveness, innovation and service delivery.
    • Building financial literacy and stewardship across the organisation by supporting leaders to make informed financial and investment decisions and effectively manage resources.
    • Developing and maintaining productive relationships with key stakeholders, including Board members, central agencies, auditors, and external service providers to support organisational objectives.
    • Leading and developing a high-performing team that fosters collaboration, accountability, continuous improvement, inclusion and professional excellence, while modelling APS Values and behaviours.
    • Contribute as an effective member of the Business Enabling Services Branch leadership team.
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    Essential:

    2.     • Demonstrated experience managing cost recovery arrangements and leading Cost Recovery Implementation Statement (CRIS) development, including financial modelling, charging frameworks, stakeholder engagement, government approval processes, and ongoing compliance with Australian Government requirements.
    • Relevant tertiary qualification in accounting, finance, economics, commerce or a related discipline.
    • Professional accounting qualifications (CA, CPA or equivalent).

Executive Level 2 - Australian Electoral Commission - Director, Event Workforce
    Field Data

    jobLocation Canberra ACT
    jobPostedDate 2026-11-02

    departmentDescription The Australian Electoral Commission (AEC) is at the heart of Australian democracy. The AEC is responsible for conducting federal elections and referendums, maintaining the Commonwealth electoral roll and making sure the Australian public is informed about all electoral matters. Our goal is to see that all eligible Australians actively participate in our democracy. The AEC values of electoral integrity through quality, agility and professionalism drive strategic and corporate planning and assist in organising the agency in a way that will effectively deliver the business into the future. The AEC is an equal opportunity employer which upholds the APS employment principles and actively encourages a diverse and inclusive workplace. The AEC’s statutory functions in relation to the conduct of elections and referendums require that the AEC and its employees must be, and must be seen to be, impartial and politically neutral. As a result, the AEC Enterprise Agreement and Political Neutrality Policy state that it is an inherent requirement of AEC employment that employees, and prospective employees, are not publicly active in any political affairs and do not publicly engage in such activities. This includes advocating any issues associated with a referendum. Should a prospective employee fail to meet this inherent requirement, they will be ineligible for employment with the AEC.
    jobDescription

    The Event Workforce Section sits within the Event Enabling Branch and is responsible for the strategic planning, recruitment, establishment, support, training coordination, and remuneration systems that enable the AEC's event workforce.


    The section provides national leadership for the attraction, recruitment, and management of the AEC's permanent election surge workforce and Temporary Election Workforce (TEW), supporting the delivery of federal elections, referendums, and by-elections across Australia.


    The section is responsible for overseeing workforce planning, labour hire arrangements, TEW systems, operational recruitment delivery, employment frameworks, training support arrangements, and workforce reporting. During a federal election, these arrangements support the engagement, training, and payment of approximately 100,000 temporary election workers nationwide.
    jobDuties

    This is an exciting opportunity to join the AEC and contribute to work that has a direct impact on Australia's democratic processes.


    As Director, Event Workforce, you will provide strategic leadership and operational management of the AEC’s workforce function, ensuring the agency can recruit, establish and support a suitably skilled temporary workforce to deliver electoral events.


    The role leads nationally significant workforce programs and services, overseeing the development of policies, procedures, systems, contracts and operational frameworks that support election readiness and successful event delivery. The Director is responsible for ensuring nationally consistent workforce management practices and service delivery across a geographically dispersed network.


    The Director works collaboratively with senior executives, State Managers, operational leaders, People Branch and external providers to deliver workforce outcomes that are scalable, compliant, efficient and capable of supporting the AEC's evolving operational requirements.


    To excel you’ll have:

    We are seeking a motivated and capable professional who can demonstrate the skills, knowledge and experience required to perform successfully in this role.

    • Demonstrated ability to provide strategic leadership in complex operational environments, including setting direction, leading teams, building organisational capability, fostering a positive workplace culture and delivering outcomes through others.
    • Demonstrated experience leading large-scale workforce mobilisation, recruitment, service delivery or operational programs, including establishing frameworks, systems and processes to support consistent national delivery.
    • Highly developed communication, stakeholder engagement and influencing skills, including experience building productive relationships, negotiating outcomes and working collaboratively across diverse stakeholder groups.
    • Demonstrated experience managing budgets, contracts, procurement activities and service provider relationships, together with the ability to evaluate risks, drive continuous improvement and deliver compliant, efficient business outcomes.

    Desirable

    • Knowledge of Commonwealth employment frameworks and workforce legislation.
    jobEligibilityRequirements
    • AEC employees must be Australian citizens.
    • Any person who is, and seen to be active in political affairs, and intends to publicly carry on this activity, may compromise the strict neutrality of the AEC and cannot be considered.
    • Applicants are required to consent to, undergo, obtain and maintain a character clearance.
    • Applicants are required to consent to, undergo, obtain and maintain the security clearance required for this role.

Executive Level 1 - Australian Criminal Intelligence Commission (ACIC) - Senior Investigator, New South Wales
    Field Data

    jobCloseDate 2026-11-12
    departmentDescription The Australian Criminal Intelligence Commission (ACIC) is Australia’s national criminal intelligence agency. Our vision is an Australia hostile to criminal exploitation. We work with our national and international partners to conduct investigations and collect intelligence to combat serious and organised crime, cybercrime and national security threats. We develop and maintain national information and intelligence sharing services and systems for use by state, territory and Commonwealth law enforcement agencies. We also deliver nationwide background checking services to support employment or entitlement decisions to keep the Australian community safe. The ACIC Board provides strategic direction to the ACIC and represents Commonwealth, state and territory law enforcement and other regulatory agencies. Our purpose is to protect Australia from criminal threats through coordinating a strategic response and collecting, assessing and disseminating intelligence and policing information.
    jobDescription


    The Opportunity


    As an ACIC Senior Investigator, you will lead the design and execution of intelligence collection plans in response to established intelligence priorities and requirements, and report the intelligence obtained through these activities. You will apply advanced collection tradecraft within authorised legislative frameworks. The role involves close collaboration with intelligence analysts at all stages of the intelligence cycle to understand intelligence gaps and requirements, inform operational planning, and to maximise the value of the intelligence obtained from collection.


    Investigators are results orientated, adept at managing risk and comfortable with partial operational autonomy. Successful applicants may be required to travel domestically or internationally. They must be able to develop productive working relationships which enhance or leverage opportunities with Law Enforcement and National Intelligence Community partners, to ensure the ACIC is positioned to deliver intelligence insights and operational outcomes.


    The investigator role requires a need to obtain special member status with the Australian Federal Police (AFP) and ability to acquire this status is an essential requirement of the position.
    jobDuties

    Role Responsibilities


    As a Senior Investigator, you will undertake the following activities to a level reflecting the standards associated with the APS EL1 level: 


    • In accordance with legislative authority, plan and lead collection activity targeting serious and organised crime (SOC) threats using a range of overt and covert capabilities, including Human Source operations, coercive powers and controlled operations, to collect and report intelligence

    • Ensure appropriate operational planning and security frameworks are applied to collection capabilities and activities

    • Contribute to communities of practice to share expertise and refine collection techniques

    • Apply collection best practice to improve the effectiveness and reliability of intelligence gathering

    • Collaborate with law enforcement, National Intelligence Community and offshore partners to influence and coordinate intelligence collection efforts, including joint operations and intelligence sharing

    • Build and maintain trusted relationships with external stakeholders to support long-term collaboration and capability development

    • Consider and deploy a range of risk mitigation strategies

    • Actively manage and document compliance activities in line with prescribed obligations


    Essential Criteria


    • Successfully complete the ACIC’s human source operations training program on commencement

    • As an employee in a designated high-risk role, complete an annual psychological assessment.

    • Must be eligible to obtain special member status and use of force with the AFP. The criteria for this requirement are having previously been a sworn police officer within the past 3 years or recent military training.


    Your Skills and Capabilities


    We invite applications from people with the following experience, skills and attributes: 


    • Demonstrated understanding of investigative and/or intelligence collection and analysis methodologies

    • Experience in implementing complex intelligence operations and/or investigations that generate unique insights into SOC or similarly complex networks

    • Ability to extract relevant information from intelligence operations and investigations to produce unique and impactful information and intelligence reports

    • Ability to understand how to operate within the context of relevant legislative, policy and legal frameworks domestically and offshore

    • Highly developed oral and written communication skills, including the ability to develop and maintain collaborative and productive internal and external working relationships

    • Proven capacity to operate as part of a multi-disciplinary team in a dynamic, agile environment to drive intelligence outcomes

    • Experience in the cultivation, recruitment and management of human sources and the effective deployment of human sources again operational objectives.
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    • Citizenship – To be eligible for employment with the ACIC, applicants must be an Australian citizen.

    • Security Clearance - The Australian community expects ACIC employees to maintain the highest levels of security and integrity. Successful applicants will be required to obtain and maintain a minimum Negative Vetting Level 1 security clearance.

    • Pre-Employment Screening – All candidates must satisfy an Organisational Suitability Assessment (OSA) prior to being offered employment at the ACIC. Please refer to the ACIC’s Character Standards for further information.

    jobDescription

    At the Office for Youth, we’re all about putting young people front and centre in shaping Australia’s future. We work with the whole of Australian Government to make sure the voices and perspectives of a broad and diverse range of young people, including First Nations young people, are heard in the development of policies and programs that affect them. From Youth Advisory Groups to a National Youth Forum, we’re creating spaces where young Australians can have a real say. Plus, we help the government get better at engaging with youth through toolkits, training, and expert advice. 


    If you’re passionate about making change and want to help amplify youth voices across the nation, come join us and be part of something exciting!


    As an Assistant Director in the Office for Youth, you will be an integral part of the leading the redesign and delivery of key youth engagement initiatives under the Australian Government’s new Youth Strategy. You will also lead training and support to build capability of youth engagement across the Australian Government, measure and evaluate the Office for Youth’s progress and support and lead child safeguarding protocols. Working closely with a dynamic team, you’ll shape how government engages with young people and ensures their voices inform the policies and decisions that affect them.

    departmentDescription The Australian Criminal Intelligence Commission (ACIC) is Australia’s national criminal intelligence agency. Our vision is an Australia hostile to criminal exploitation. We work with our national and international partners to conduct investigations and collect intelligence to combat serious and organised crime, cybercrime and national security threats. We develop and maintain national information and intelligence sharing services and systems for use by state, territory and Commonwealth law enforcement agencies. We also deliver nationwide background checking services to support employment or entitlement decisions to keep the Australian community safe. The ACIC Board provides strategic direction to the ACIC and represents Commonwealth, state and territory law enforcement and other regulatory agencies. Our purpose is to protect Australia from criminal threats through coordinating a strategic response and collecting, assessing and disseminating intelligence and policing information.

    departmentDescription The Australian Electoral Commission (AEC) is at the heart of Australian democracy. The AEC is responsible for conducting federal elections and referendums, maintaining the Commonwealth electoral roll and making sure the Australian public is informed about all electoral matters. Our goal is to see that all eligible Australians actively participate in our democracy. The AEC values of electoral integrity through quality, agility and professionalism drive strategic and corporate planning and assist in organising the agency in a way that will effectively deliver the business into the future. The AEC is an equal opportunity employer which upholds the APS employment principles and actively encourages a diverse and inclusive workplace. The AEC’s statutory functions in relation to the conduct of elections and referendums require that the AEC and its employees must be, and must be seen to be, impartial and politically neutral. As a result, the AEC Enterprise Agreement and Political Neutrality Policy state that it is an inherent requirement of AEC employment that employees, and prospective employees, are not publicly active in any political affairs and do not publicly engage in such activities. This includes advocating any issues associated with a referendum. Should a prospective employee fail to meet this inherent requirement, they will be ineligible for employment with the AEC.
    jobDescription

    The Delivery and Support Branch plays a key role in supporting the AEC’s mission by leading the preparation and delivery of electoral services.


    The Branch is responsible for the preparation and delivery of critical electoral operations and works closely with stakeholders to deliver outcomes that support the integrity, effectiveness and continuous improvement of Australia’s electoral system. There are currently four teams across the Delivery and Support Branch, including the Event Management and Delivery Assurance team, Event Technology team, Polling and Count Operations team and Voter Services team.
    jobDuties

    The Delivery and Support Branch is looking to engage multiple Assistant Directors across the Polling and Count Operations and Voter Services sections. These are exciting opportunities to join the AEC and contribute to work that has a direct impact on Australia's democratic processes.


    As the AEC progresses towards its target level of event readiness, these roles will play a critical role in strengthening workforce capability, supporting operational readiness and ensuring the agency is well positioned to deliver future electoral events while maintaining business-as-usual operations.


    Polling and Count Operations

    The Assistant Director, Polling and Count Operations will work closely with the Director and staff to administer key federal election operational doctrine, including policies, standard operating procedures and election procedure handbooks.


    Voter Services

    The Assistant Director, Voter Services will work closely with the Director and staff to manage operational and logistical projects that support the delivery of the remote and mobile polling services program, including developing project plans, timelines and deliverables. The Voter Services section manages voting services for electors who are unable to attend a polling place or pre-poll voting centre during electoral events. These services include mobile polling, postal voting, overseas voting, remote polling, defence voting for the ADF, and telephone voting.

    jobDuties

    The Role

    The EL1 Product Manager within Maritime Inventory Management leads the delivery of integrated product outcomes across contract management and repairable item functions, supporting sustainment of common inventory for Navy capability. Operating with a high degree of independence, the role plans and manages workflows, resources and priorities to ensure effective contract performance, materiel availability and value for money outcomes.


    The position is accountable for overseeing contract governance and supplier performance, while also leading the planning and coordination of repairable item activities, including forecasting, induction, repair and disposal decisions. It requires the application of logistics, commercial and product management principles to balance cost, risk and performance outcomes.


    The EL1 leads and develops a multidisciplinary team, provides strategic advice and maintains strong relationships with internal stakeholders and industry partners. The role ensures alignment of activities with Maritime Sustainment Division’s objectives and contributes to continuous improvement and sustainment outcomes across the supply chain.


    About our Team

    Maritime Sustainment Division (MSD) operates within the Naval Shipbuilding and Sustainment Group, delivering in service sustainment of naval capability in support of Navy’s operational readiness. As part of MSD’s transformation to the Maritime Sustainment Model, the Maritime Inventory Management Directorate has been established to centralise and modernise inventory management across the fleet.


    The Maritime Inventory Management Directorate is responsible for the procurement and management of common inventory, repairable items and cross class equipment, ensuring the right materiel is available to support maintenance delivery, sustain onboard allowances and enable future fleet operations.


    Our team works across procurement, logistics, engineering and operational stakeholders to deliver integrated supply chain outcomes, improve efficiency through scale, and drive cost savings through aggregated purchasing and reduced duplication, while providing assured inventory support that directly underpins Navy capability and readiness in a dynamic and evolving operational environment.


    Our Ideal Candidate

    The EL1 Product Manager in the Maritime Inventory Management Directorate is a highly capable leader who performs effectively in a complex sustainment environment and demonstrates a strong commitment to Defence’s values of service, courage, respect, integrity and excellence. You bring strong experience within product, contract and logistics management disciplines, demonstrating the ability to integrate multiple functions to deliver reliable materiel outcomes.


    You are confident leading multidisciplinary teams, setting priorities and managing workflows across contract management and repairable item activities. You exercise sound judgement to balance cost, risk and performance, ensuring value for money and effective supplier performance.


    You are an effective communicator who builds and sustains productive relationships with internal and external stakeholders, and can influence outcomes in a complex environment. You demonstrate strategic thinking, strong analytical skills and a focus on continuous improvement to deliver outcomes aligned with the Maritime Inventory Management Directorate objectives and Navy capability requirements.
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    Security Clearance

    Applicants must be able to obtain and maintain a security clearance at “Negative Vetting Level 1”.

    departmentDescription Sport Integrity Australia leads the Australian government’s efforts to protect sport against threats to its integrity – doping, competition manipulation and the mistreatment and abuse of sports participants. Sport Integrity Australia is the cornerstone of the government's comprehensive sport integrity strategy: Safeguarding the Integrity of Sport. Our role is to provide advice and assistance to counter the use of prohibited substances and methods in sport, stop the abuse of children and other persons in a sporting environment, cease manipulation of sporting competitions and to protect members of sporting organisations and other persons in a sporting environment from bullying, intimidation, discrimination or harassment. Sporting organisations, and other stakeholders, benefit from the ability to deal with a single nationally coordinated organisation to address all sport integrity issues. Sport Integrity Australia offers guidance on integrity matters to sports that don’t have developed integrity capabilities. Our focus is policy development, intelligence, investigations (primarily of doping cases), education, outreach and capability building. We are Australia's National Anti-Doping Organisation.
    jobDescription

    Our Opportunity

    The Cyber Security Engineer is responsible for designing, implementing, maintaining and improving the agency's cyber security controls, services and technologies. The role supports the protection of agency information, systems and data through the application of security engineering principles, technical expertise and risk-based approaches aligned to the Australian Government Information Security Manual (ISM), Protective Security Policy Framework (PSPF) and Essential Eight.

    Operating with a high degree of autonomy, the EL1 Cyber Security Engineer provides technical leadership on cyber security initiatives, manages security technologies, supports incident response activities, and contributes to the agency's cyber security uplift program.

    The role requires strong stakeholder engagement skills and the ability to translate complex technical matters into practical advice for both technical and non-technical audiences.


    An Individual Flexibility Agreement (IFA) will be considered for the successful applicant.


    ﻿Security Engineering and Architecture

    • Design, implement and maintain cyber security controls across cloud, on-premises and hybrid environments.
    • Develop secure architecture patterns and technical standards.
    • Review and assess ICT projects and system designs to ensure compliance with security requirements.
    • Provide security-by-design advice for new technologies and services.
    • Contribute to secure configuration baselines and hardening standards.


    Security Operations

    • Manage and optimise security technologies including: 
      • Microsoft Defender Suite
      • Microsoft Sentinel
      • Microsoft Entra ID
      • Microsoft Intune
      • Firewalls and secure gateways
      • Endpoint Detection and Response (EDR) solutions
      • Vulnerability management tools
    • Monitor security events and support threat detection activities.

    About us

    Australian Government Solicitor (AGS) is the Australian Government's central legal practice and trusted legal adviser. As part of the Attorney-General's Department, AGS advises and represents the Commonwealth on some of Australia's most complex, significant and high-profile legal matters.


    Operating as a fee-for-service legal practice, AGS competes with private sector providers for the majority of its work while delivering legal services that support the national interest. 


    With more than 550 lawyers and approximately 800 professionals across Australia, AGS combines the scale and sophistication of leading private practice with the opportunity to contribute to outcomes that shape government, public administration and the development of Australian law.


    Practice Services is a corporate area within AGS that provides a range of practice support functions, including the provision of internal and external training.


    Why join AGS?

    At AGS, you will have the opportunity to develop your career while contributing to outcomes that matter to Australia


    • National impact: AGS contributes to outcomes on legal matters that influence government decision-making, public policy and the administration of law across Australia
    • Professional growth: Work alongside recognised experts and access ongoing professional development, mentoring and career progression opportunities
    • Flexible working: Benefit from flexible and hybrid working arrangements that support both professional and personal commitments
    • Competitive employment conditions: Enjoy an attractive remuneration package, 15.4% superannuation and generous leave entitlements.


    Few legal practices can offer the opportunity to provide support on matters that combine legal complexity, public impact and direct engagement with government decision-makers.

    departmentDescription The Department of Parliamentary Services (DPS) supports Australia’s Parliament and parliamentarians through innovative, unified and client focused services.     jobDescription

    The Department of Parliamentary Services is seeking experienced cyber security professionals to join our Cyber Security Branch in the Parliamentary Executive Level 1 leadership roles:

     

    Assistant Director, Cyber Intelligence and Awareness


    Assistant Director, Cyber Security Governance and Assurance


    These roles offer a unique opportunity to apply your expertise at the heart of Australia’s democracy, helping protect the people, information, systems and services that support the Australian Parliament.

     

    About the Department

     

    The Department of Parliamentary Services supports the functions of the Australian Parliament and the work of parliamentarians by providing professional services, advice, facilities and secure technology. The department is also the custodian of Australian Parliament House, a nationally significant building, a working parliamentary environment and an important destination for Australian and international visitors.

     

    Our diverse workforce provides services including information and communications technology, cyber and physical security, library and research services, broadcasting, Hansard, property services, visitor engagement and corporate support.

     

    Working with us means contributing directly to the secure and effective operation of Parliament in a complex, high-profile and dynamic environment.

     

    About the Cyber Security Branch

     

    The Cyber Security Branch protects the department and Australian Parliament House from evolving cyber threats. The branch works across operations, intelligence, awareness, vulnerability management, governance, risk and assurance to strengthen cyber resilience, support informed decision-making and enable the safe delivery of parliamentary services.

     

    Our work requires strong technical knowledge, sound judgement and trusted engagement with executives, parliamentarians, business areas, technology teams, service providers and Australian Government security partners.


    The Opportunities


    Assistant Director, Cyber Intelligence and Awareness

     

    This role leads a multidisciplinary capability spanning cyber threat intelligence and cyber security awareness.

     

    You will translate intelligence, vulnerability and human-risk insights into practical, prioritised action.

     

    You will lead the development of threat assessments, executive briefings, security awareness initiatives and vulnerability remediation activities that directly strengthen the department’s cyber resilience.

     

    Key responsibilities include:

     

    • leading and developing a multidisciplinary cyber security team
    • establishing intelligence requirements and producing strategic, operational and tactical cyber threat assessments
    • delivering intelligence-led cyber security advice and support for overseas travel
    • leading targeted awareness, training, behaviour-change and simulated phishing programs
    • overseeing the identification, prioritisation and remediation of enterprise vulnerabilities
    • providing authoritative advice to senior executives on threats, vulnerabilities and human cyber risk
    • establishing meaningful performance measures and executive reporting
    • building trusted relationships across parliamentary departments, government, industry and intelligence-sharing partners. 


    This opportunity would suit an experienced cyber security leader who can connect intelligence, technology and human behaviour to influence decisions and deliver measurable risk reduction.

     

    Assistant Director, Cyber Security Governance and Assurance

     

    This role supports the delivery and continuous improvement of the department’s cyber security governance, compliance and assurance functions.

     

    You will coordinate governance and assurance activities, maintain policies and control frameworks, monitor compliance obligations and prepare clear reporting for senior executives and governance committees.

     

    The role operates in alignment with the Protective Security Policy Framework, Information Security Manual and Essential Eight.

     

    Key responsibilities include:

     

    • coordinating cyber security governance and assurance activities
    • developing and maintaining policies, standards, procedures and control baselines
    • coordinating assurance schedules, evidence collection, control assessments and findings management
    • supporting internal and external audit and assurance activities
    • monitoring Essential Eight and PSPF maturity uplift
    • preparing executive briefs, reports and committee papers
    • maintaining accurate, traceable and audit-ready governance records
    • engaging business and technology stakeholders to implement agreed outcomes
    • identifying control gaps and recommending practical improvements
    • supervising and supporting staff and contractors as required. 

     

    This opportunity would suit a governance and assurance professional who combines attention to detail with strong analytical, stakeholder engagement and executive communication skills.


    Who we are looking for

      

    Across both roles, we are looking for people who can:

     

    • lead people and deliver outcomes in a complex operating environment
    • exercise sound judgement, autonomy, integrity and accountability
    • translate complex cyber security issues into clear and practical advice
    • build productive relationships across technical and non-technical areas
    • manage competing priorities and sensitive information
    • develop high-quality briefs, reports and governance products
    • contribute to a collaborative, inclusive and high-performing culture.

     

    Applicants should demonstrate expertise relevant to the role for which they are applying. 

     

    Relevant tertiary qualifications, cyber security certifications or substantial professional experience will be highly regarded.

    departmentDescription We are Australia’s only national institution focused exclusively on the diverse history, cultures, and heritage of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander Australia. We house the world’s most important and extensive collection of cultural information and material and we provide leadership, publish and promote greater understanding and appreciation of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples. We create opportunities for people the world over to engage with and be transformed by the knowledge, the culture and the story of Australia’s Indigenous peoples. We are Australia’s national institution for leadership in Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander research. We care for a priceless collection, including films, photographs, video and audio recordings as well as the world’s largest collection of printed and other resource materials for Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander studies. We undertake and encourage scholarly, ethical, community-based research in a variety of sectors, including health, native title, languages and education. Our publishing house, Aboriginal Studies Press, regularly publishes outstanding writing that promotes Australian Indigenous cultures. Our activities affirm and raise awareness of the richness and diversity of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander cultures and histories.
    jobDescription

    About the Group

    The Office of the CEO (OCEO) is at the forefront of the Institute, liaising with external stakeholders including, but not limited to: Ministerial Offices, Commonwealth and state and Territory Departments; including the National Indigenous Australians Agency (NIAA), the Department of Foreign Affairs and Trade (DFaT) and the Office of the Arts (OfTA) as well as other Indigenous Portfolio Bodies, the Australian Diplomatic Corp, relevant Embassies and Cultural and other Institutions both domestically and abroad.


    About the Program Area

    AIATSIS is leading a program to secure the return of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples’ cultural heritage held overseas to its original custodians and owners or appropriate cultural institutions.

    Returning material to country for purposes of cultural revitalisation is a key aspiration of Indigenous communities. The Return of Cultural Heritage (RoCH) program supports the cultural resurgence of Australia’s First Nations peoples. This initiative will strengthen the signal both to the nation and globally that Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander culture is respected, celebrated and valued.

    The program will rely on expert knowledge as well as strong community engagement and partnerships, and a network of engaged individuals and organisations to achieve its goals.


    Role Summary

    The Warlpiri Assistant Director is responsible for the development of research strategies, conducting high-quality research and evaluation of outcomes, producing reports, official return requests, policy papers and other outputs. They are responsible for engagement with Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples, communities and organisations, as well as domestic and international cultural institutions and collectors.

    This role is funded through external (corporate) funding sources with a primary focus to work in partnership with the Warlpiri communities impacted by the Granites gold mines, supporting the delivery of community-centred initiatives, building strong relationships, and helping to advance priorities that contribute to positive social, cultural and economic outcomes.

    The position provides support to the Director in informing the legislative requirements and the development of changes to repatriation policy, practice and guidelines.
    jobDuties

    • assist the Director through the preparation of written articles for a variety of publications and presentations, and the preparation of media content.

    Affirmative Measures positions

    Jobs in the APS are open to all Australian citizens. There are some jobs, though, that are open only to specific members of the Australian population. This practice is known as an Affirmative measure and is a targeted strategy to ensure the diversity of the APS workforce reflects the diversity of the Australian population.  


    Any job can be restricted to Aboriginal and/or Torres Strait Islander people only, and it doesn't necessarily have to involve working on Indigenous issues. If a position has been advertised as an Affirmative measure, it will be clearly marked as such.


    The use of the Indigenous Affirmative Measures provision at AIATSIS allows for targeted recruitment of Indigenous Australians into the organisation. If you are applying for an Affirmative Measures position, you will need to indicate your Aboriginal and/or Torres Strait Islander heritage on our application form and provide confirmation if required. 


    What is Cultural Capability? 

    As all positions at AIATSIS are ‘Identified’ each position will have various elements of involvement with the delivery of Indigenous specific services, programs, and policies as part of AIATSIS core functions. AIATSIS therefore needs to also identify the cultural knowledge; skills and personal attributes needed to deliver the required outcomes.


    Every part of how we work, who we work with and for, requires varying levels of knowledge of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander cultures, protocols and an understanding of issues that enable effective communication and ultimately effective performance in the role. To assist you in determining the required cultural capabilities of this role, you can refer to the Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander Cultural Capability – A Framework for Commonwealth Agencies

    departmentDescription The Bureau of Meteorology is one of the few organisations that touches the lives of all Australians and all Australia, every day. The Bureau works across Australia and remote islands, providing services from the Antarctic to beyond the equator, and from the Indian Ocean to the Pacific. We are Australia's national weather, climate and water agency, in the Agriculture, Water and Environment portfolio of the Australian Government, operating under the authority of the Meteorology Act 1955 and the Water Act 2007. We provide data, information, knowledge, insight and wisdom to help Australians prepare and respond to the realities of their natural environment, including droughts, floods, fires, storms, tsunami and tropical cyclones. Our products and services include observations, forecasts, analysis and advice covering Australia's atmosphere, water, oceans and space environments. We undertake focused scientific research in support of our operations and services. Through regular forecasts, warnings, monitoring and advice, we provide one of Australia's most fundamental and widely used public services. We have strong relationships with our customers, partners and stakeholders in Australia, including the Australian Community and the emergency services sectors, all-levels of Government, and focus sectors including aviation, agriculture, energy and resources, national security and water.
    jobDescription

    This role is responsible for leading the design, development, and delivery of scalable, secure, and high-performing data solutions that support key organisational initiatives and strategic outcomes. Operating within a modern, cloud-enabled data environment, the position provides technical leadership across data engineering practices, ensuring alignment with enterprise data strategy, architecture standards, and governance requirements.


    The role drives the implementation and optimisation of data pipelines and integration processes (batch and real-time), leveraging the organisation’s enterprise data platform (including Cloudera) to enable reliable data ingestion, storage, and processing. It plays a critical part in shaping platform capability by evaluating emerging technologies and continuously enhancing engineering standards, patterns, and delivery practices.


    Working collaboratively with business stakeholders, architects, and cross-functional teams, the position translates complex business requirements into fit-for-purpose data solutions and supports end-to-end delivery of data products. The role also contributes to DevSecOps practices, automation, and observability, ensuring solutions are robust, production-ready, and aligned with compliance, security, and governance frameworks.


    In line with APS Executive Level 1 expectations, the position requires strong leadership, sound judgement, and the ability to manage competing priorities in a fast-paced environment. It also focuses on building organisational capability through mentoring, fostering continuous improvement, and maintaining strong stakeholder relationships to ensure data platforms meet current and future needs.

    • Lead the design and delivery of data solutions to support key delivery initiatives, ensuring alignment with enterprise data strategy, enterprise architecture standards, and organisational priorities.

    • Design, implement and maintain data solutions leveraging the Bureau’s Cloudera-based enterprise data platform, ensuring scalable, secure and governed data ingestion, storage and processing; and provide expert advice on, and evaluate, emerging technologies and tooling to ensure data platforms remain fit-for-purpose and aligned to future organisational needs.
