    departmentDescription The Australian Criminal Intelligence Commission (ACIC) is Australia’s national criminal intelligence agency. Our vision is an Australia hostile to criminal exploitation. We work with our national and international partners to conduct investigations and collect intelligence to combat serious and organised crime, cybercrime and national security threats. We develop and maintain national information and intelligence sharing services and systems for use by state, territory and Commonwealth law enforcement agencies. We also deliver nationwide background checking services to support employment or entitlement decisions to keep the Australian community safe. The ACIC Board provides strategic direction to the ACIC and represents Commonwealth, state and territory law enforcement and other regulatory agencies. Our purpose is to protect Australia from criminal threats through coordinating a strategic response and collecting, assessing and disseminating intelligence and policing information.
    jobDescription


    The Opportunity


    As an ACIC Senior Investigator, you will lead the design and execution of intelligence collection plans in response to established intelligence priorities and requirements, and report the intelligence obtained through these activities. You will apply advanced collection tradecraft within authorised legislative frameworks. The role involves close collaboration with intelligence analysts at all stages of the intelligence cycle to understand intelligence gaps and requirements, inform operational planning, and to maximise the value of the intelligence obtained from collection.


    Investigators are results orientated, adept at managing risk and comfortable with partial operational autonomy. Successful applicants may be required to travel domestically or internationally. They must be able to develop productive working relationships which enhance or leverage opportunities with Law Enforcement and National Intelligence Community partners, to ensure the ACIC is positioned to deliver intelligence insights and operational outcomes.


    The ACIC is undergoing legislation change. Investigator roles may require a need to obtain special member status (exercising powers of a police constable) in the medium term; however, this should not prohibit interest in these roles at the current time, despite it being listed in the essential criteria.
    jobDuties

    Role Responsibilities


    As a Senior Investigator, you will undertake the following activities to a level reflecting the standards associated with the APS EL1 level: 


    • In accordance with legislative authority, plan and lead collection activity targeting serious and organised crime (SOC) threats using a range of overt and covert capabilities, including Human Source operations, coercive powers and controlled operations, to collect and report intelligence

    • Ensure appropriate operational planning and security frameworks are applied to collection capabilities and activities

    • Contribute to communities of practice to share expertise and refine collection techniques

    • Apply collection best practice to improve the effectiveness and reliability of intelligence gathering

    • Collaborate with law enforcement, National Intelligence Community and offshore partners to influence and coordinate intelligence collection efforts, including joint operations and intelligence sharing

    • Build and maintain trusted relationships with external stakeholders to support long-term collaboration and capability development

    • Consider and deploy a range of risk mitigation strategies

    • Actively manage and document compliance activities in line with prescribed obligations


    Essential Criteria


    • Successfully complete the ACIC’s human source operations training program on commencement

    • As an employee in a designated high-risk role, complete an annual psychological assessment.

    • Must be eligible to obtain special member status and use of force with the AFP. The criteria for this requirement are having previously been a sworn police officer within the past 3 years or recent military training.


    Your Skills and Capabilities


    We invite applications from people with the following experience, skills and attributes: 


    • Demonstrated understanding of investigative and/or intelligence collection and analysis methodologies

    • Experience in implementing complex intelligence operations and/or investigations that generate unique insights into SOC or similarly complex networks

    • Ability to extract relevant information from intelligence operations and investigations to produce unique and impactful information and intelligence reports

    • Ability to understand how to operate within the context of relevant legislative, policy and legal frameworks domestically and offshore

    • Highly developed oral and written communication skills, including the ability to develop and maintain collaborative and productive internal and external working relationships

    • Proven capacity to operate as part of a multi-disciplinary team in a dynamic, agile environment to drive intelligence outcomes

    • Experience in the cultivation, recruitment and management of human sources and the effective deployment of human sources again operational objectives
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    • Citizenship – To be eligible for employment with the ACIC, applicants must be an Australian citizen.

    • Security Clearance - The Australian community expects ACIC employees to maintain the highest levels of security and integrity. Successful applicants will be required to obtain and maintain a minimum Negative Vetting Level 1 security clearance.

    • Pre-Employment Screening – All candidates must satisfy an Organisational Suitability Assessment (OSA) prior to being offered employment at the ACIC. Please refer to the ACIC’s Character Standards for further information.

Executive Level 1 - National Anti-Corruption Commission - Senior Investigator
    Field Data
    departmentName National Anti-Corruption Commission

    departmentDescription The National Anti-Corruption Commission (NACC) is an independent Commonwealth agency that commenced in 2023. The NACC mission is to enhance integrity in the Commonwealth public sector by deterring, detecting and preventing corrupt conduct involving Commonwealth public officials through education, monitoring, investigation, reporting and referral.
    jobDescription

    About the National Anti-Corruption Commission


    The NACC enhances integrity in the Commonwealth public sector by deterring, detecting, and preventing corrupt conduct involving Commonwealth public officials. It does this through education, monitoring, investigation, reporting and referral.


    Working with us

    This is an exciting time to join the NACC. We are looking for outstanding individuals to lead our corruption investigations and deliver timely outcomes to enhance integrity in the public service.


    NACC staff work in a complex and dynamic setting where exceptional judgement and an ability to achieve results is critical.


    Successful candidates will have the ability to cooperate, manage, lead and perform across corporate and operational functions.


    About the Role


    Reporting to the Director Operations and under broad guidance, the Senior Investigator is responsible for the conduct of complex investigations into allegations of corrupt conduct, using the full range of investigative methodologies, including coercive powers available under the National Anti-Corruption Commission Act 2022 (Cth) (NACC Act). 
    jobDuties

    In accordance with the Executive Level 1 Work Level Standards, you will:


    • Lead the planning and ongoing management of corruption investigations, including record keeping, to ensure robust and timely outcomes.
    • Facilitate effective working relationships within a multidisciplinary team environment, comprising investigators, intelligence analysts, lawyers, and other specialists.
    • Undertake and/or assist in all relevant investigative activities, including: 
      • identify relevant lines of enquiry and prepare investigation plans for complex investigations.
      • collect evidence including drafting decision minutes and NACC Act notices, preparing affidavits to obtain warrants, and conducting interviews.
      • oversee the analysis of large and complex data sets of information. 
      • preparing investigation reports, obtaining witness statements, hearing briefs and briefs of evidence, and giving evidence in court.
    • Provide advice to the Commissioner and senior executives of the NACC in relation to the ongoing progress and outcomes of corruption investigations. 
    • Ensure effective working relationships and governance of investigations being jointly undertaken with partner agencies.
    • Represent the interests of the NACC and helping manage the NACC’s relationships with relevant stakeholders including other Australian Government agencies, State and Territory representatives and the private sector as necessary.
    • Lead by example and develop, coach and mentor more junior staff. This may include direct line management of APS6 investigators.
    • Undertake and contribute to the continuous improvement of the investigative function within the NACC, including; developing, reviewing, evaluating and implementing relevant policies, procedures, processes, tools and best practices.
    • Lead and exemplify a healthy and safe working environment, modelling and promoting ethical behaviour and practices consistent with the APS Code of Conduct. 
    • On occasion, undertake travel throughout Australia (including regional and remote areas), as operationally required. 


    The NACC is a small agency. In addition to these activities, from time to time you may be asked to undertake tasks that are outside your usual span of duties but that are within the range of your capabilities. Supporting one another in this way provides opportunities for exposure across the agency and is an integral part of the NACC’s positive workplace culture.   
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    Employment with the NACC is subject to the following conditions:


    Citizenship – Applicants must be an Australian Citizen.

    Security Clearance – This is a Designated Security Assessment Position (DSAP). The occupant of this position must obtain and retain an AGSVA security clearance to the specified level.

    Suitability assessment – The NACC must be satisfied that successful applicants are of suitable character. They must undergo a rigorous pre-employment suitability screening process that can be intrusive in nature including (but not limited to) detailed background, character, employment, police, and financial checks.

    Probation - A probation period will apply to any new ongoing engagement to the Australian Public Service.



    Eligibility (Prescribed Qualifications)/Other Requirements:


    • Demonstrated experience in a similar role.
    • Diploma in Government Investigations (or the ability to obtain within 12 months of commencement).
    • Legal background will be highly regarded but not essential.
    • Undertake any necessary training as required from time to time pertaining to the exercising of powers as an Authorised Officer under the NACC Act or other requirements of the role.
    • Due to the nature of the role, you may at times be exposed to objectionable/undesirable material, either directly or indirectly and must be aware of this possibility.

    • Given the nature of the NACCs operational activities, employees may be involved in matters where their identity or involvement becomes know to affected persons, witnesses or external stakeholders. Employees should be aware that anonymity cannot be guaranteed, notwithstanding the NACCs commitment to managing employee safety, security and wellbeing risks.

Executive Level 2 - Australian National Audit Office - EL2 Senior Director - SADA
    Field Data
    departmentName Australian National Audit Office
    departmentURL https://www.anao.gov.au/

    jobLocation Barton ACT
    jobPostedDate 2026-10-23
    jobCloseDate 2026-11-15
    departmentDescription The Australian National Audit Office (ANAO) is a specialist public sector entity that provides a full range of audit services to the Australian Parliament, the Australian Government and public sector entities. The 'Auditor-General Act 1997' provides the legislative framework for the role of the Auditor-General and the ANAO. The purpose of the ANAO is to drive accountability and transparency in the Australian Government sector through quality evidence based audit services and independent reporting to Parliament, the Executive and the public, with the result of improving public sector performance. The Executive is accountable to Parliament for its use of public resources and the administration of legislation passed by the Parliament. The Auditor-General scrutinises and provides independent assurance as to whether the Executive is operating and accounting for its performance in accordance with Parliament’s purpose.
    jobDescription

    Who we are


    The Australian National Audit Office (ANAO) purpose is to support accountability and transparency in the Australian Government sector through independent reporting to the Parliament and thereby contribute to improved public sector performance.


    At the ANAO, you will make meaningful and valuable contributions to public sector administration through a full range of audit and assurance services.


    The ANAO is located in Canberra and has approximately 450 employees. Our people come from a range of disciplines and backgrounds, many with commerce, accounting, finance or economics qualifications, but increasingly this has expanded to include public policy and administration, management, law, social sciences, and information technology.


    Systems Assurance and Data Analytics (SADA) Group provides IT audit and data analytics support to the ANAO's assurance, performance and performance statements audit work and other information reports.


    The ANAO is a professional organisation of curious and critical thinkers, with strong analytical capabilities, underpinned by the values of integrity, respect and excellence. Our people are technical and analytical experts in their field and are driven to produce high quality outcomes. We have a unique opportunity to make a difference and support accountability and transparency and improved public sector performance. 


    The opportunity


    As an EL2 Senior Director, you will provide leadership, oversight and strategic direction for SADA’s system assurance, AI, cyber and data audits. You will drive capability development and manage high-risk, high-impact engagements.


    You will experience, lead and shape unique and diverse work of varying complexity in both large and small agencies. The broad experience, support and development opportunities available in SADA - including technical training, L&D courses and networking opportunities will directly support you to be effective and successful in this role.
    jobDuties

    SADA work is varied and unique, duties can include but are not limited to:

    • Lead audit teams across multiple entities delivering complex systems, cyber, AI and data assurance audits.
    • Provide advice on emerging technology risks.
    • Oversee capability and quality uplift across digital auditing disciplines.
    • Manage senior stakeholder relationships and governance responsibilities.
    • Drive innovation, resource planning and quality outcomes.

    Please note:

    • This recruitment process is being used to fill both ongoing and non-ongoing positions. Non-ongoing positions will be offered on a specified term basis for an initial period of up to 12 months with the possibility of being extended.
    • A merit pool will be created from this activity that may be used to fill similar vacancies for a period of 18 months from the date of advertising.
    • All positions are based in Canberra, with flexible working arrangements where the employee works partly in the office and partly from home, as agreed with the manager.

    Who we are looking for


    We are committed to providing an environment that values diversity and supports employees to reach their full potential.


    To be successful in this role, you will demonstrate:

    • Demonstrated senior leadership in auditing or data analytics, preferably system, cyber or data assurance.
    • Ability to influence organisational direction on emerging risks.
    • Strong strategic thinking, communication and stakeholder engagement skills.

    Desirable qualifications


    There are no mandatory qualifications for this role, however, the following are highly desirable:

    • Relevant tertiary qualifications in IT, cyber, data analytics, accounting, auditing or related fields.
    • Demonstrated interest in auditing emerging technologies.
    • Qualifications or demonstrated on-the-job experience in a discipline that requires analytical skills.
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    Citizenship – To be eligible for employment with the ANAO, applicants must be an Australian citizen.


    Security Clearance – The Australian community requires the highest level of integrity from ANAO employees. The preferred applicant will be required to obtain and maintain a security clearance at the Baseline Level. For more information about the AGSVA security clearance process, please refer to the AGSVA website.

    Assessment process / Reasonable adjustment


    As part of the assessment process, you may be asked to participate in one or more of the following assessment activities:

    • Interview (in-person or virtual)
    • Reference Check

    If you believe you may require reasonable adjustments to participate in the assessment process, please ensure this is indicated on your application. A member of our recruitment team will contact you to discuss the adjustments prior to the assessment process.


    Use of Artificial Intelligence (AI) in recruitment


    The ANAO supports candidates using artificial intelligence (AI) tools and technologies to assist in the preparation of their application. To support a fair, transparent and merit-based assessment process, candidates are required to disclose any use of AI in preparing their application. This includes (but is not limited to) AI-based writing assistants, resume builders, or other generative AI tools.


    All applications must remain an accurate and genuine representation of your skills, experience and capability, and you must be able to explain and substantiate your submission if required.


    Candidates are expected to read and understand the APSC Principles for candidate use of AI in recruitment, which outline expectations for appropriate use of AI, including maintaining authenticity and avoiding misleading or unfair use. Failure to appropriately disclose AI use, or use of AI in a misleading way, may impact your assessment.


    How to apply


    Applications must be submitted using the online recruitment system. Applications sent via email cannot be accepted.


    Applicants are required to submit a current CV/resume and a statement of claims (500 words maximum) outlining why you are interested in this position and why you believe that you have the skills, capabilities, knowledge and experience to be considered for this role. Please provide examples which demonstrate your ability to perform the duties of the position.


    In preparing your application, you should consider the ANAO’s capability framework and provide relevant examples that demonstrate your skills and experience against these areas and how they align to the role. You can access the ANAO Capability Framework for further guidance on the capabilities and behaviours expected.


    Please note: Your email is our primary method of contacting you - please make sure the email address you provide us is correct, current and accessible.


    We strongly recommend that you use a personal email address rather than your work email address to ensure you don’t miss updates and invitations to participate in the assessment process. Please also regularly check your junk or spam folders.


    We look forward to receiving your application and exploring how your skills and experience can contribute to our agency.

Executive Level 1 - Australian Criminal Intelligence Commission (ACIC) - Senior Investigator, South Australia
    Field Data
    departmentName Australian Criminal Intelligence Commission (ACIC)
    departmentURL www.acic.gov.au
    jobClassification Executive Level 1
    jobName Senior Investigator, South Australia
    Salary $121,756 to $146,676
    applicationURL https://www.acic.gov.au/about-us/careers
    jobLocation Adelaide SA
    jobPostedDate 2026-10-23
    jobCloseDate 2026-11-08
    departmentDescription The Australian Criminal Intelligence Commission (ACIC) is Australia’s national criminal intelligence agency. Our vision is an Australia hostile to criminal exploitation. We work with our national and international partners to conduct investigations and collect intelligence to combat serious and organised crime, cybercrime and national security threats. We develop and maintain national information and intelligence sharing services and systems for use by state, territory and Commonwealth law enforcement agencies. We also deliver nationwide background checking services to support employment or entitlement decisions to keep the Australian community safe. The ACIC Board provides strategic direction to the ACIC and represents Commonwealth, state and territory law enforcement and other regulatory agencies. Our purpose is to protect Australia from criminal threats through coordinating a strategic response and collecting, assessing and disseminating intelligence and policing information.
    jobDescription


    The Opportunity


    As an ACIC Senior Investigator, you will lead the design and execution of intelligence collection plans in response to established intelligence priorities and requirements, and report the intelligence obtained through these activities. You will apply advanced collection tradecraft within authorised legislative frameworks. The role involves close collaboration with intelligence analysts at all stages of the intelligence cycle to understand intelligence gaps and requirements, inform operational planning, and to maximise the value of the intelligence obtained from collection.


    Investigators are results orientated, adept at managing risk and comfortable with partial operational autonomy. Successful applicants may be required to travel domestically or internationally. They must be able to develop productive working relationships which enhance or leverage opportunities with Law Enforcement and National Intelligence Community partners, to ensure the ACIC is positioned to deliver intelligence insights and operational outcomes.


    The investigator role requires a need to obtain special member status with the Australian Federal Police (AFP) and ability to acquire this status is an essential requirement of the position.
    jobDuties

    Role responsibilities


    As a Senior Investigator, you will undertake the following activities to a level reflecting the standards associated with the APS EL1 level: 


    • In accordance with legislative authority, plan and lead collection activity targeting serious and organised crime (SOC) threats using a range of overt and covert capabilities, including Human Source operations, coercive powers and controlled operations, to collect and report intelligence

    • Ensure appropriate operational planning and security frameworks are applied to collection capabilities and activities

    • Contribute to communities of practice to share expertise and refine collection techniques

    • Apply collection best practice to improve the effectiveness and reliability of intelligence gathering

    • Collaborate with law enforcement, National Intelligence Community and offshore partners to influence and coordinate intelligence collection efforts, including joint operations and intelligence sharing

    • Build and maintain trusted relationships with external stakeholders to support long-term collaboration and capability development

    • Consider and deploy a range of risk mitigation strategies

    • Actively manage and document compliance activities in line with prescribed obligations


    Essential Criteria


    • Successfully complete the ACIC’s human source operations training program on commencement

    • As an employee in a designated high-risk role, complete an annual psychological assessment.

    • Must be eligible to obtain special member status and use of force with the AFP. The criteria for this requirement are having previously been a sworn police officer within the past 3 years or recent military training.


    Your Skills and Capabilities


    We invite applications from people with the following experience, skills and attributes: 


    • Demonstrated understanding of investigative and/or intelligence collection and analysis methodologies

    • Experience in implementing complex intelligence operations and/or investigations that generate unique insights into SOC or similarly complex networks

    • Ability to extract relevant information from intelligence operations and investigations to produce unique and impactful information and intelligence reports

    • Ability to understand how to operate within the context of relevant legislative, policy and legal frameworks domestically and offshore

    • Highly developed oral and written communication skills, including the ability to develop and maintain collaborative and productive internal and external working relationships

    • Proven capacity to operate as part of a multi-disciplinary team in a dynamic, agile environment to drive intelligence outcomes

    • Experience in the cultivation, recruitment and management of human sources and the effective deployment of human sources again operational objectives
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    • Citizenship – To be eligible for employment with the ACIC, applicants must be an Australian citizen.

    • Security Clearance - The Australian community expects ACIC employees to maintain the highest levels of security and integrity. Successful applicants will be required to obtain and maintain a minimum Negative Vetting Level 1 security clearance.

    • Pre-Employment Screening – All candidates must satisfy an Organisational Suitability Assessment (OSA) prior to being offered employment at the ACIC. Please refer to the ACIC’s Character Standards for further information. 
    jobCategory Intelligence
    jobContact Vic
    jobContactPhone 0418 328 583
    jobNotes

    The ACIC is committed to diversity and inclusion. We celebrate and champion diversity to ensure we reflect the community we serve, and pride ourselves on our vibrant culture. We welcome applications from Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples, women, people with disability, people from culturally and linguistically diverse backgrounds, those who identify as LGBTIQ+, neurodiverse, mature aged employees and carers.

    departmentDescription Our purpose is to provide culturally safe and affordable accommodation for First Nations people who need to be away from home to access services and economic opportunities. Across our network of hostels, dedicated AHL staff provide accommodation and meals for residents in a culturally safe and supportive environment. We have a strong network of relationships with other organisations, including First Nations organisations. This network both refers residents to AHL and works alongside AHL to support residents to meet their needs. We are proud to be one of the largest employers of First Nations people in the Australian Public Service, First Nations employees make up over 40% of our workforce. Working with us will provide you with a chance to contribute to improving the quality of life and economic opportunities for First Nations people.

    • Setting direction and taking accountability for operational performance, service delivery and outcomes.

    • Strong strategic thinking, risk management and problem-solving skills. 
    • Highly developed stakeholder engagement and influencing skills, including the ability to build trusted relationships with First Nations communities. 
    • A commitment to culturally responsive service delivery and continuous improvement.

    The Role

    Your role will be to lead the Directorate of Materiel Protection where you will develop, implement and oversee a range of policy and technology programs protecting Australia’s most sensitive sovereign technologies.


    As Director Materiel Protection:

    • You will have a leading role in developing and implementing policies and programs to protect technologies and systems critical to Australia’s national security.
    • You can expect to develop and manage integrated business and technical programs across whole of capability, whole of lifecycle, whole of enterprise. Establish policy, doctrine, and authoritative knowledge frameworks. Engage across Defence, Agencies, and Industry to deliver outcomes.
    • You will coordinate and integrate high-level Electronic, Cyber and Systems Engineering processes across a suite of new and developing technology programs.
    • Your day will consist of engineering, technology, and security reviews, program management, stakeholder engagement, team leadership and building culture across a small expert team.


    About our Team

    The Directorate of Materiel Protection leads a small team of specialists within Technology and Security Branch in the Defence Delivery Group. As Director Materiel Protection, you will work closely with other directorates within the Engineering Technology & Materiel Logistics Division, the broader DDG and with government and industry stakeholders. Your team will deliver Materiel Protection specific outcomes as part of our integrated professional engineering, cyber and logistics service offering.


    Our Ideal Candidate

    We are looking for:

    • A professional engineer or technologist who is committed to delivering high-quality outcomes and achieving objectives within an established performance framework.
    • A thoughtful and innovative practitioner who can contribute to the development and implementation of secure-by-design technology protection approaches across the broad Defence ecosystem.
    • Proven experience in Engineering, Cyber, Security, Procurement, and Business management.
    • A leader who can achieve complex goals and outcomes with a small expert team, and across a broad Defence, Government and Industry stakeholder group.
    • Please note the successful candidate will be required to gain and maintain a Positive Vetting level security clearance.

As a Positive Vetting clearance is required, applicants must be Australian Citizens; be able to account for their personal background for their whole life; and be able to demonstrate suitability to work in a high security intelligence environment.

    jobDescription

    The Principal Intelligence Analyst is the senior intelligence specialist and operational delivery lead for the Regulatory Intelligence team. Working under the direction of the Assistant Director, the role leads execution of the Regulatory Intelligence Strategy through the coordinated delivery of timely, objective and actionable intelligence products.


    The role exercises operational authority over intelligence intake, triage, sequencing and analytical delivery. It leads complex and high-priority analytical work, integrates analytical workstreams, provides authoritative quality assurance and advice, and supports the embedding of agreed intelligence governance, standards and methodologies. The role provides professional leadership, mentoring and task coordination, but does not have primary responsibility for staff performance management, workforce allocation or formal team leadership.
    jobDuties
    • Lead execution of agreed Regulatory Intelligence Strategy priorities by converting intelligence requirements into coordinated analytical activities, products and delivery timeframes.
    • Lead complex Strategic Intelligence Assessments and other high-priority intelligence products, including defining analytical approaches, directing contributing workstreams and ensuring judgements are evidence-based and defensible.
    • Oversee the end-to-end flow of intelligence work, including intake, triage, sequencing, dependencies, milestones and the redirection of analytical effort in response to emerging intelligence and changing priorities.
    • Provide authoritative quality assurance and peer review to ensure intelligence products are clearly expressed, appropriately caveated and fit for executive and regulatory decision-making.
    • Integrate intelligence from regulatory activity, internal data, stakeholder engagement, open sources and partner agencies, and translate findings into practical implications, options and recommended actions.
    • Build trusted relationships and provide professional leadership, coaching and mentoring while embedding agreed governance, standards, methodologies, product frameworks and capability initiatives.
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    To be successful in this role you will need to demonstrate the following:


    • Demonstrated experience leading complex intelligence analysis and producing high-quality strategic or operational intelligence products for executive, regulatory or risk-based decision-making.
    • Excellent research, conceptual thinking, analytical and problem-solving skills, including sound judgement when evaluating incomplete, ambiguous, sensitive or contested information and applying structured analytical techniques.
    • Strong ability to execute agreed strategic priorities through coordinated analytical delivery, including triaging requirements, directing analytical effort, integrating workstreams and managing competing demands and dependencies.
    • Excellent written and oral communication skills, including concise and influential briefings and rigorous quality assurance for senior and diverse audiences.
    • Strong stakeholder engagement and professional leadership skills, including building productive relationships, negotiating priorities, coordinating specialist effort, mentoring staff and constructively challenging assumptions.
    • Strong regulatory intelligence and data literacy, integrity and professional courage, including appropriate handling of sensitive information and operation within legislative, policy and information-governance requirements.

    departmentDescription The Australian Radiation Protection and Nuclear Safety Agency (ARPANSA) is the Australian Government's primary authority on radiation protection and nuclear safety. Our purpose is to protect the Australian people and the environment from the harmful effects of radiation, through understanding risks, best practice regulation, research, policy, services, partnerships and engaging with the community.
    jobDescription

    The Australian Radiation Protection and Nuclear Safety Agency (ARPANSA) is the Australian Government's primary authority on radiation protection and nuclear safety. Our purpose is to protect the Australian people and the environment from the harmful effects of radiation, through understanding risks, best practice regulation, research, policy, services, partnerships and engaging with the community.


    The Governance and Strategic Engagement Section leads ARPANSA’s governance and strategic engagement functions. It supports the agency’s relationships with broader Government, international partners, industry and other key stakeholders. The section also oversees enterprise risk, quality management, corporate planning, performance monitoring, project governance and mandatory reporting, including reporting to Parliament. It provides policy advice and coordination across the agency, supports engagement with ARPANSA’s Minister and their Office, and serves as the primary liaison with the Department of Health, Disability and Ageing on matters relating to its policy oversight of ARPANSA and the ARPANS Act.


    Why work for us?


    • Opportunities to engage with national and international stakeholders
    • Inclusive and diverse workplace
    • Competitive salaries with 15.4% superannuation
    • Generous leave conditions
    • Flexible work practices
    • Modern amenities and free parking at both our Yallambie and Miranda sites.
    jobDuties

    Primary purpose of the role

    In this role, your primary responsibility will be contributing to the delivery of the Agency’s International engagement strategy; liaison with national and international counterparts; and facilitating the agency’s involvement in international radiation protection, nuclear safety and security matters.


    You will be expected to prepare a broad range of highly complex and sensitive policy documentation including international strategies, stakeholder engagement plans, and ensure that ARPANSA meets its obligations under relevant international safety and security frameworks.


    While it is preferable that the role is based at our Melbourne office (Yallambie), consideration will be given to candidates who could work from Sydney or Canberra.


    Key accountabilities

    • Plan, manage, coordinate and undertake the delivery of ARPANSA’s international engagement strategy.
    • Drive ARPANSA’s engagement with international partners and ensure they produce value for ARPANSA and cements ARPANSA’s reputation as a best practise regulator of radiation protection and nuclear safety.
    • Support the co-ordination of major international agreements where ARPANSA is the lead agency, such the Convention on Nuclear Safety, the Joint Convention, and the Codes of Conduct.
    • Liaise with the relevant Australian Government departments and agencies in a manner that progresses ARPANSA’s objectives.
    • Coordinate and provide expert advice to stakeholders on matters related to the protection of people and the environment from the harmful effect of radiation.
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    Our ideal candidate - job specific capabilities

    Our ideal candidate is someone who has a passion for engaging international stakeholders as well as working with other Commonwealth Government Departments and Agencies. While it isn’t essential to have a background in the field of radiation or nuclear safety, ideally, you will have experience working with technical experts to communicate effectively with a non-technical audience.


    To be considered for this position, you will need a relevant degree from an Australian tertiary institution or comparable overseas qualifications and/or substantial related experience appropriate to the role and duties of the position. You will possess highly developed liaison, consultation, and stakeholder engagement skills, with the ability to cultivate and maintain productive working relationships while undertaking strategic or operational negotiations with other Australian agencies.


    You must demonstrate the ability to undertake research and analysis to provide high-level advice to senior management and broader government stakeholders. Additionally, you will exhibit highly developed written communication skills and be able to communicate effectively with a broad range of stakeholders, including the Minister's Office, Department officials, and scientific and technical colleagues.


    You must hold Australian citizenship and be prepared to travel interstate from time to time as required. ARPANSA requires all new employees to obtain and maintain a national security clearance as a condition of engagement. You must also meet required pre-employment checks as necessary. 


    Selection criteria

    The selection criteria for this role are:

    1. Demonstrated ability to provide high-level advice to senior management in order to inform/contribute to Government policy.
    2. Demonstrated ability to build and leverage strategic relationships across government to influence outcomes and support policy objectives.
    3. Demonstrated ability to foster mutually agreeable outcomes with international stakeholders in a politically sensitive environment, and in line with Australian Government policy.
    4. Evidence of effective time-management and ability to manage multiple complex and competing priorities while exercising sound judgement.
    5. Demonstrated ability to interpret, apply and provide strategic advice on relevant international and Australian Government legislative and policy frameworks.


    Eligibility

    Employment with ARPANSA is subject to: 

    • conditions prescribed within the Public Service Act 1999
    • agency and role specific pre-employment security checks that may include, but are not limited to:
      • entitlement to work in Australian
      • national criminal history
      • international criminal history
      • professional memberships
      • qualifications
      • sanctions
      • anti-money laundering & counter-terrorism financing
      • ASIC banned & disqualified persons
      • ASIC credit registered persons
      • bankruptcy & national personal insolvency
      • directorship
      • global media check
    • former Australian Government employer information sharing
    • Protective Security Policy Framework (PSPF) security clearance eligibility requirements
    • evidence of citizenship status required
    • evidence of qualifications required of the role
    • internal security assessment 
    • security clearance vetting
    • the CEO of ARPANSA, or their Delegate, approving a Notice of Engagement.

    The Australian Government Security Vetting Agency (AGSVA) website provides detailed information about the requirements for obtaining a security clearance at the required level, the documentary evidence you will be required to provide, the forms that need to be completed, and the relevant timeframes. Please ensure that you are able to provide the required evidence if you apply for the position. Failure or refusal to complete the required forms, or to comply with reasonable requests from AGSVA, will result in ARPANSA withdrawing your security clearance application, and your employment with the agency not progressing.


    ARPANSA is responsible for assessing how information relates to the agency’s security risks, as well as a person’s suitability for employment by the agency. ARPANSA will use the information you provide to undertake relevant checks in order to identify any factors or circumstances that pose a security risk to the agency and assess whether you are suitable to have access to the agencies non-public assets. You will be notified if you are assessed as unsuitable for employment by the agency.

    The opportunity we have available


    The Consumer Safeguards, Media and Connectivity Division (CSMCD) is at the centre of developing policy and programs to ensure all Australians have access to safe and reliable connectivity and communications services, as well as ensuring the diversity of Australia’s media landscape. We also focus on digital inclusion for Australia’s First Nations communities.


    The Inclusion, News and Media Diversity Branch in CSMCD is looking for a high-performing Director to lead our First Nations Programs team responsible for the delivery of First Nations digital inclusion measures, recommended by the First Nations Digital Inclusion Advisory Group (the Advisory Group) and funded in the 2024-25 Budget, and coordination of departmental work related to the National Agreement on Closing the Gap – Target 17, Outcome 17 and Priority Reforms.


    The First Nations Programs team has been established to take forward delivery of the First Nations digital inclusion Budget measures, including $20 million to deliver free community Wi-Fi in remote communities through a contestable program, $6 million towards improving the national collection of data on First Nations digital inclusion, and $22 million to establish a First Nations Digital Support Hub ($4 million) and a Network of Digital Mentors ($18 million).


    Our work is supported by the Digital Inclusion team in the branch has primary responsibility for providing policy and secretariat support to the Advisory Group, as well as oversight of digital inclusion policy, and digital inclusion initiatives for First Nations people consistent with achieving Target 17, which aims for equal levels of digital inclusion for First Nations people by 2026. 
    jobDuties

    What you will do


    The role is varied and dynamic, as the responsible Director you will:


    • provide strong, collaborative and supportive leadership within your section and the branch
    • lead in the development, implementation and delivery of policy and programs to support First Nations digital inclusion and Closing the Gap Outcome 17 and Target 17 objectives
    • support the work of other sections across the branch on First Nations digital inclusion initiatives, including the team responsible for the secretariat function of the First Nations Digital Inclusion Advisory Group
    • undertake research and analysis on priority issues, particularly those affecting First Nations people, (including general research or economic and data analysis, depending on your skills and experience)
    • develop persuasive communications materials, including written reports, briefing papers, speech inputs and discussion papers
    • lead on and assist with project strategy, planning, implementation and coordination
    • undertake stakeholder engagement, including close collaboration with agencies and colleagues across the public service, state and territory governments, industry and First Nations communities
    • work closely with the Advisory Group and communities to maximise the outcomes and benefits of funded initiatives for First Nations people   
    • develop and assist with formal advice to the Executive and Government, such as Cabinet submissions and proposals for new policies and programs.

     

    Inherent requirements for the role


    To be considered for this position, you must demonstrate the following capabilities evaluated against the Executive Level 2 Work level standards:


    • Strategic capability: Strong experience in leading policy reform and program delivery, with the ability to drive the development and implementation of improved socio-economic outcomes for First Nations people, thorough effective grant management and procurement processes.
    • Negotiation and collaboration: Proven high-level negotiation skills with the capability to partner with internal and external stakeholders (including state and territory officials) to ensure strong, efficient and effective implementation of First Nations programs.
    • Reporting and governance: Strong analytical capacity to coordinate reporting across First Nations programs, maintaining framework integrity and Indigenous data sovereignty principles.
    • Communication and influence: Superior written and oral communication skills, with a track record of producing high-level executive briefings and successfully influencing diverse senior stakeholder groups.
    • People and culture leadership: Proven capacity to lead teams with commitment, taking responsibility for shaping team culture, developing staff capability, steering workflows under tight deadlines, and making an active and positive contribution to the culture and leadership of your work area and the department.


    Desirable requirements


    Experience in the telecommunications sector would be considered highly favourable, including an understanding of telecommunications networks and technologies such as community Wi-Fi and broadband network architectures and services.


    Cultural capability requirement


    The department believes every role contributes to cultural safety and that building staff cultural capability helps us deliver better policies, programs and services for First Nations people and the broader Australian community. Candidates will be required to demonstrate their willingness to develop their cultural capability as part of the assessment process. In making it a requirement across all roles in our department, we are taking a practical step in delivering our commitments under the Our Stories on Country Agreement and the Diversity, Equity and Inclusion Strategy 2025–28.


    As an EL2 you will be expected to:


    • Provide strategic leadership to embed cultural capability across programs, policies or functions.
    • Influence senior leaders on culturally informed approaches.
    • Drive partnerships and co-design with Aboriginal and/or Torres Strait Islander organisations and communities where applicable or appropriate.
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    Eligibility requirements


    This is an Affirmative Measures vacancy – This vacancy is open only to Aboriginal and/or Torres Strait Islander people. 


    The filling of this vacancy is intended to constitute an Affirmative Measure under section 8(1) of the Racial Discrimination Act 1975. To be eligible to apply under the Affirmative Measures initiative, applicants must: 


    • be an Aboriginal person and/or a Torres Strait Islander person 
    • identify as an Aboriginal person and/or a Torres Strait Islander person, and 
    • be accepted by their community as being an Aboriginal person and/or a Torres Strait Islander person 

     

    For more information please visit - Affirmative measure for recruiting Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander Australians: A guide for applicants.


    Employment with the Department of Infrastructure, Transport, Regional Development, Communications, Sport and the Arts is subject to conditions prescribed within the Public Service Act 1999 including: 


    • Citizenship: candidates must be an Australian citizen at the time of submitting your application. 
    • Health Assessment: The preferred candidate may be required to undergo a medical examination conducted by the department’s preferred medical provider.
    • Security Clearance: The successful candidate must be able to obtain and/or maintain a security clearance at Baseline Level. You must be willing to disclose all relevant and required information. You must have lived in Australia, or have a checkable background, for at least the preceding five years for Baseline Vetting clearances. More information on the security clearance vetting process is available on the Australian Government Security Vetting Agency (AGSVA) website.
    • Police check: The successful candidate must complete an Australian Federal Police criminal history check.
    • Integrity and Performance check: The successful candidate must satisfy the requirements in an Integrity and Performance check which is completed by your most recent employer.

    departmentDescription The Australian Criminal Intelligence Commission (ACIC) is Australia’s national criminal intelligence agency. Our vision is an Australia hostile to criminal exploitation. We work with our national and international partners to conduct investigations and collect intelligence to combat serious and organised crime, cybercrime and national security threats. We develop and maintain national information and intelligence sharing services and systems for use by state, territory and Commonwealth law enforcement agencies. We also deliver nationwide background checking services to support employment or entitlement decisions to keep the Australian community safe. The ACIC Board provides strategic direction to the ACIC and represents Commonwealth, state and territory law enforcement and other regulatory agencies. Our purpose is to protect Australia from criminal threats through coordinating a strategic response and collecting, assessing and disseminating intelligence and policing information.
    jobDescription

    The Senior Software Engineer will be responsible for defining and shaping the development and improvement of software development processes, standards and tools for the ACIC. Responsibilities vary across projects and involve the application of strong leadership and management skills to ensure employees are productive and supported in the drive for quality outcomes. This includes the ACIC’s software development and implementation projects and Business-as-Usual activities, as well as sustainment of the ACIC’s broader service delivery objectives.
    jobDuties

    Role Responsibilities


    As a Senior Software Engineer, you will: 


    • Provide strategic leadership and direction, inspiring and guiding a multi-disciplinary team to achieve organisational objectives.

    • Plan, prioritise and coordinate competing activities to ensure deadlines and business priorities are effectively managed.

    • Communicate persuasively and engage effectively with stakeholders to build and maintain productive internal and external working relationships.

    • Apply critical thinking, analysis and problem-solving skills to identify, evaluate and implement solutions to complex business problems

    • Contribute to the development of high-quality briefings, papers, submissions and correspondence for internal and external decision-making forums.

    • Use strategic thinking and evidence-based decision-making to drive improvements in software quality, development productivity, and maintainability through effective tools and processes.

    • Provide cross-team leadership and support to enable the successful delivery, deployment, operation, and maintenance of software services through the coordination and continuous improvement of shared platforms, environments, deployment pipelines, tooling, processes, and dependencies.


    Your Skills and Capabilities


    We invite applications from people with the following experience, skills and attributes: 


    • Demonstrated ability to lead or coordinate end to end delivery and sustainment activities, including incident and problem resolution, defect remediation, testing, change control and production release.

    • Demonstrated experience in supporting and developing software systems, in particular in environments based on Java, JEE frameworks, Containerisation (Kubernetes), Oracle, Unix, Windows, HTML5, CSS, Typescript, Javascript, Tomcat, Node, React and Spring Boot framework.

    • Demonstrated experience in supporting or integrating software products from third party vendors, including modified and bespoke applications, commercial or government off-the-shelf.

    • Well-developed management, stakeholder engagement, documentation and prioritisation skills, with the judgement to balance delivery, risk, compliance and system reliability in a complex ICT setting.

    • Tertiary ICT qualifications or equivalent/demonstrated experience are highly regarded.
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    • Citizenship – To be eligible for employment with the ACIC, applicants must be an Australian citizen.

    • Security Clearance - The Australian community expects ACIC employees to maintain the highest levels of security and integrity. Successful applicants will be required to obtain and maintain a minimum Negative Vetting Level 1 security clearance.

    • Pre-Employment Screening – All candidates must satisfy an Organisational Suitability Assessment (OSA) prior to being offered employment at the ACIC. Please refer to the ACIC’s Character Standards for further information.

Executive Level 1 - Australian War Memorial - Registrar

    departmentDescription The Australian War Memorial combines a shrine, a world-class museum, and an extensive archive. The Memorial's purpose is to commemorate the sacrifice of those Australians who have died in war or on operational service and those who have served our nation in times of conflict. Its mission is leading remembrance and understanding of Australia’s wartime experience.
    jobDescription

    The Registrar manages and leads three teams within Registration: the Acquisitions team, the Loans & Exhibitions team and Storage, Movement & Assurance team. The role oversees the operations of three Assistant Registrars and their teams who manage incoming donations, defence transfers, purchases, cultural gifts, deaccessioning, incoming and outgoing loans of collection, gallery showcase access and changeovers, collection assurance, storage and movement. The Registrar works directly to the Head of Collection Services and contributes to the delivery of core collection management functions of the Memorial’s National Collection. The position works in close partnership with curatorial and conservation sections in the National Collection Branch and sets the priorities and work program for the Registration team.
    jobDuties
    • Leading the operations and functions of the Registration team within the Memorial.
    • Fostering cohesive teams and leading staff to achieve results and deliver organisational outcomes.
    • Advising on collection management strategies, policies, and procedures, and leading their development and implementation.
    • Provide mentoring, advice, information and training to the Registration team, and identify, analyse and resolve issues arising from the team’s responsibilities in support of the Memorial’s collection management program.
    • Providing accurate and timely management reports and analysis on the collection and its storage.
    • Working collaboratively with Memorial sections to build positive relationships and partnerships with internal and external stakeholders.
    • Provide advice and expertise on legal and ethical frameworks relating to collection management including compliance with relevant legislation and leading industry practice. 
    • Abide by and promote the principles and practices of Work Health and Safety as outlined in the WHS Act as it applies to personal safety and that of the team. Take a leading role in collection hazard management and the development of compliant procedures and policies.
    • Contribute to Collection Services team outcomes for the safe, collaborative and effective management of the National Collection.
    • Lead and contribute to a healthy and functioning workplace culture, upholding the APS Code of Conduct and the Memorial’s Values and Behaviours.

    Desirable Qualifications and Experience


    • Qualifications in Museum Studies, Cultural Heritage Management, Collection Management, History, Archaeology, Arts, or a related field will be highly regarded but are not essential.
    • 5+ years’ experience in museum registration, collection management, or cultural heritage operations or a related field will be highly regarded but is not essential.
    jobEligibilityRequirements
    • To be eligible for employment with the Australian War Memorial you must be an Australian citizen as per section 22(8) of the Public Service Act 1999.
    • All applicants external to AWM that are offered employment will be required to successfully undergo a National Police Check, even if they currently hold a security clearance. The check is conducted in accordance with the Australian Government Protective Security Policy Framework requirements.

    Salary $125,863 to $143,171

    jobLocation Canberra ACT
    jobPostedDate 2026-10-19
    jobCloseDate 2026-11-08
    departmentDescription The Clean Energy Regulator is the Government body responsible for administering legislation to reduce carbon emissions and increase the use of clean energy. The Clean Energy Regulator has administrative responsibilities for the: • National Greenhouse and Energy Reporting Scheme, under the National Greenhouse and Energy Reporting Act 2007 • Emissions Reduction Fund, under the Carbon Credits (Carbon Farming Initiative) Act 2011, • Renewable Energy Target, under the Renewable Energy (Electricity) Act 2000, and • Australian National Registry of Emissions Units, under the Australian National Registry of Emissions Units Act 2011, • Nature Repair Market, under the Nature Repair Act 2023, • Guarantee of Origin, under the Future Made in Australia (Guarantee of Origin) Bill 2024. Our purpose is to accelerate carbon abatement for Australia. We work with our stakeholders and clients including Australian Government departments and agencies, industry bodies, liable entities and the community to provide regulatory services of the highest standard. We offer challenging and rewarding work in administering legislation that will reduce carbon emissions and increase the use of clean energy. Our agency is committed to workplace diversity and aims to create an environment that values and utilises the contribution of people from different backgrounds, experiences and perspectives. We encourage applications from Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander People, people with a disability, people from diverse cultural and linguistic backgrounds and mature age workers
    jobDescription

    Acknowledgment of Country


    The Clean Energy Regulator acknowledges the Traditional Custodians of the lands on which we live and work across Australia. We pay our respects to Elders past and present and extend that respect to all Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples.


    Yuma – Welcome to the Clean Energy Regulator

    The Clean Energy Regulator plays a key role in driving carbon abatement and supporting the transition to clean energy in Australia by administering national schemes that measure, reduce and offset emissions. 


    Through initiatives such as the Australian Carbon Credit Unit (ACCU) Scheme, we support projects that reduce emissions, store carbon and create economic opportunities across Australia - including on land and waters where Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples have rights, interests and deep cultural connections. 


    We recognise the importance of ensuring Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander voices, perspectives and knowledge are reflected in our work, particularly where projects intersect with Country, culture and community.


    We warmly invite applications from Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples for this opportunity. We value the strength, lived experience, cultural knowledge and community connection that Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples bring, and the important contribution this makes to delivering effective, inclusive and respectful outcomes for Australia.


    About this Recruitment – Affirmative Measure


    This role is being recruited under the Affirmative Measures – Indigenous Employment provision.


    This means the opportunity is open only to Aboriginal and/or Torres Strait Islander applicants. The use of this measure supports the Australian Government’s commitment to increasing representation of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples in the APS and addressing historical under representation.


    We are committed to a fair and inclusive process where all candidates are assessed on merit, with an understanding that capability can be demonstrated in many ways, including through community, cultural, and lived experience.


    As a part of the recruitment process, you will be required to provide evidence that confirms your Aboriginal and/or Torres Strait Islander heritage. This is generally via a letter issued by a recognised Aboriginal and/or Torres Strait Islander organisation that confirms:


    • You are of Aboriginal and/or Torres Strait Islander descent; or,
    • You identify as an Aboriginal and/or Torres Strait Islander person; and
    • You are accepted by the community as such.


    If you progress to the interview stage, you will be required to provide this evidence prior to your interview.


    Our Commitment to Cultural Safety


    We are committed to creating a culturally safe and respectful recruitment process and workplace.


    In this recruitment process, we will:


    • Use clear, plain language and minimise unnecessary jargon
    • Provide flexibility in how you demonstrate your skills and experience
    • Offer alternative assessment approaches (such as storytelling or conversational approaches where appropriate)
    • Ensure panel members are culturally aware and respectful
    • Provide opportunities to ask questions and feel comfortable throughout the process


    We recognise that everyone communicates differently and want to create an environment where you can best show your strengths and potential.


    Our Commitment to Ongoing Support


    We are committed to supporting you throughout your time at the Clean Energy Regulator by fostering a workplace where you feel culturally safe, valued and connected. Our First Nations Network plays a central role in this by creating an inclusive environment for Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander staff, amplifying First Nations voices, and supporting initiatives led by First Nations peoples. Through celebrating and sharing cultural heritage, recognising significant events and milestones, and promoting cultural understanding across the agency, the Network helps strengthen our ongoing commitment to reconciliation and respect.


    Our Role and Functions


    The agency is undergoing a period of significant change and growth, with new responsibilities and expanded scope of schemes, such as the Cheaper Home Batteries initiative, Guarantee of Origin scheme and the Nature Repair Market. The agency has also been appointed as the preferred regulator for distributed energy products, also known as consumer energy resources.


    With a variety of roles on offer we are searching for candidates who can demonstrate capability in a variety of different areas.


    Information about all the schemes we administer, as well as our publications, can be found on our website.


    Scheme Operations Division


    Our Scheme Operations Division enables entry and participation in the schemes administered by the agency. Staff assess applications and make statutory decisions on client scheme entitlements. The division works collaboratively across the agency to ensure efficient and effective administration of all schemes.


    Scheme Support Division


    The Scheme Support Division supports the agency to resolve complex issues, advocate for legislative and policy change, screen and monitor client behaviour, address serious non-compliance and improve operational efficiency and effectiveness.


    Consumer energy national technical regulator

    The Australian Government, in partnership with state and territory governments, has developed a national framework for regulating consumer energy resources. We are preparing to take on the role of the Consumer Energy Resources National Technical Regulator under the framework, subject to legislation.


    The new regulatory framework will give consumers greater confidence in their devices, helping them get more value from their energy systems. The framework sets out roles for us as the Consumer Energy National Technical Regulator, including registering suppliers, installers and products; collecting information about installed devices; and monitoring compliance.


    Current Opportunities


    There are immediate and anticipated vacancies available across a range of sections within the agency.


    Immediate Vacancies


    Carbon Markets branch


    Immediate vacancies are available within the Environmental Markets Analysis section and the Renewable Energy Markets section in the Carbon Markets branch.


    The role of the Carbon Markets branch is to provide authoritative, evidence-based insights on economic carbon markets to support effective outcomes for the schemes the Clean Energy Regulator (CER) administers and provide strategic policy analysis and support for the agency. The branch analyses all of the schemes administered by the CER.


    The Environmental Markets Analysis section delivers authoritative, evidence-based insights on Australia’s environmental markets, specifically the Safeguard Mechanism, Australian Carbon Credit Unit, Nature Repair Market, and Product Guarantee of Origin schemes, undertakes research and analysis on industrial decarbonisation, the Safeguard Mechanism, and transition to a net zero economy. The team delivers the environmental markets analysis for the CER’s flagship market intelligence publication, the Quarterly Carbon Market Report, and builds, maintains and enhances in-house models of the Australian Carbon Credit Unit and Safeguard Mechanism Scheme.


    The Renewable Energy Market Analysis section provides data, analysis and insights on renewable electricity markets administered by the CER, including the Renewable Energy Target, the Renewable Electricity Guarantee of Origin and the Consumer Energy National Technical Regulator. The section delivers evidence-based analysis on market trends, certificate markets and technology uptake of large-and small-scale renewable technologies and storage, supporting the Quarterly Carbon Market Report, policy development and decision-making.


    The Strategic Policy and Analysis section is responsible for the delivery of high-quality insights and analysis on emerging policies and events relevant to the Clean Energy Regulator. It supports the work of the agency by providing research and advice on complex issues, particularly in respect of interactions across schemes or involving new policy developments. Some key responsibilities are delivery of strategic briefs to the Board on topics of interest and developing resolutions to cross-scheme issues and interactions.


    Anticipated Vacancies


    Geospatial, Analytics and Reporting section


    Anticipated future opportunities may arise for Geospatial and Data Specialists within the Geospatial, Analytics and Reporting section.


    The Geospatial, Analytics and Reporting section supports Australian Carbon Credit Unit Scheme data and geospatial analysis capabilities that deliver reporting and analytical products to support compliance monitoring, scheme assurance and internal and external reporting.


    Anticipated future opportunities may also arise for Geospatial and Data Specialists supporting the Australian Carbon Credit Unit Scheme, and for Data Analysts within the Data Innovation and Tools section.


    The merit pool from this process may also be used to fill roles within the Renewable Energy Target scheme and National Greenhouse and Energy Reporting scheme.
    jobDuties

    As an Executive Level 1 you will be expected to undertake work that is complex in nature and operates with a high level of autonomy and accountability. You will manage a defined body of work or lead a small team, exercising sound judgement and initiative to deliver outcomes aligned with organisational priorities. You will be capable of managing competing priorities, providing authoritative and well-reasoned advice, and representing the work area with stakeholders. Many roles involve people leadership responsibilities, including setting direction, managing workflows, developing staff capability and monitoring performance.


    This recruitment stream covers a range of specialist roles. Individual positions may focus on data analytics, geospatial analysis, market analysis, economic and policy analysis, research, reporting or data management, and candidates are not expected to possess expertise across all disciplines.


    Depending on the role, duties may include:


    • Provide authoritative written and verbal advice to senior stakeholders by translating complex economic and policy analysis, spatial patterns, or research findings into clear options, recommendations, implications and risks.
    • Lead very complex data, geospatial, market intelligence, policy or economic analysis activities under broad direction, exercising judgement, independence and accountability for quality outcomes.
    • Set priorities, work plans and performance expectations for a team, project or analytical work program; monitor delivery through others and adjust priorities as risks, demand or evidence changes.
    • Lead complex problem solving by integrating evidence from internal and external datasets, spatial information, scheme or operational data, research and stakeholder intelligence.
    • Oversee governance, assurance and decision quality for analytical products, including data standards, metadata, peer review, traceability, auditability, privacy, secrecy, ethical use of data and defensible documentation.
    • Manage and influence key stakeholder relationships, including other government agencies, users of analysis, data custodians, technical specialists, policy and compliance teams, market bodies or other external partners, to support sound decision-making and implementation.
    • Drive innovation and business improvement across analytics, geospatial, reporting, market intelligence, data management or market monitoring processes, including use of tools, automation, quality controls and improved end-user products.
    • Manage team capacity, capability and wellbeing; coach staff, provide performance feedback and build capability in analytical reasoning, GIS/spatial methods, economic and market analysis, data governance and communication of insights.
    • Identify and manage risks for the work program, including information security, data quality, analytical integrity, reputational risk, market sensitivity, delivery risk and appropriate escalation and controls.
    • Specific roles and responsibilities will be discussed with candidates as they progress through the recruitment process. If an offer of employment is made to you, you will receive the detailed duties for the specific role being offered.


    Key Capabilities and Attributes

    We are seeking candidates who demonstrate some of or all our core capabilities:


    • Building Relationships
    • Developing Self and Others
    • Managing Risk
    • Applying Legislation and Policy
    • Delivering Results
    • Leveraging Insights


    In addition to these core capabilities, you will also have some of or all the following personal attributes:


    • Leads work through ambiguity, setting direction and prioritising delivery 
    • Translates policy and legislative intent into coherent, implementable regulatory frameworks 
    • Acts as a trusted focal point, providing clear, balanced advice to stakeholders 
    • Builds and maintains strategic relationships across the energy, technology and regulatory sectors 
    • Models APS and CER values by fostering a collaborative, inclusive team culture


    Tertiary qualifications are not required for these roles, however qualifications in or progress towards the following would be advantageous:


    • Data, Analytics and Geospatial
    • Public policy and government
    • Science and Engineering
    • Commerce and economics

If you are offered a role and do not already hold a security clearance at or above level applicable to the position, you will need to undertake the security clearance process before you can commence employment.
