    The role of the Carbon Markets branch is to provide authoritative, evidence-based insights on economic carbon markets to support effective outcomes for the schemes the Clean Energy Regulator (CER) administers and provide strategic policy analysis and support for the agency. The branch analyses all of the schemes administered by the CER.


    The Environmental Markets Analysis section delivers authoritative, evidence-based insights on Australia’s environmental markets, specifically the Safeguard Mechanism, Australian Carbon Credit Unit, Nature Repair Market, and Product Guarantee of Origin schemes, undertakes research and analysis on industrial decarbonisation, the Safeguard Mechanism, and transition to a net zero economy. The team delivers the environmental markets analysis for the CER’s flagship market intelligence publication, the Quarterly Carbon Market Report, and builds, maintains and enhances in-house models of the Australian Carbon Credit Unit and Safeguard Mechanism Scheme.


    The Renewable Energy Market Analysis section provides data, analysis and insights on renewable electricity markets administered by the CER, including the Renewable Energy Target, the Renewable Electricity Guarantee of Origin and the Consumer Energy National Technical Regulator. The section delivers evidence-based analysis on market trends, certificate markets and technology uptake of large-and small-scale renewable technologies and storage, supporting the Quarterly Carbon Market Report, policy development and decision-making.


    The Strategic Policy and Analysis section is responsible for the delivery of high-quality insights and analysis on emerging policies and events relevant to the Clean Energy Regulator. It supports the work of the agency by providing research and advice on complex issues, particularly in respect of interactions across schemes or involving new policy developments. Some key responsibilities are delivery of strategic briefs to the Board on topics of interest and developing resolutions to cross-scheme issues and interactions.


    Anticipated Vacancies

    ﻿

    Geospatial, Analytics and Reporting section


    Anticipated future opportunities may arise for Geospatial and Data Specialists within the Geospatial, Analytics and Reporting section.


    The Geospatial, Analytics and Reporting section supports Australian Carbon Credit Unit Scheme data and geospatial analysis capabilities that deliver reporting and analytical products to support compliance monitoring, scheme assurance and internal and external reporting.


    Anticipated future opportunities may also arise for Geospatial and Data Specialists supporting the Australian Carbon Credit Unit Scheme, and for Data Analysts within the Data Innovation and Tools section.


    The merit pool from this process may also be used to fill roles within the Renewable Energy Target scheme and National Greenhouse and Energy Reporting scheme.
    jobDuties

    As an Executive Level 1 you will be expected to undertake work that is complex in nature and operates with a high level of autonomy and accountability. You will manage a defined body of work or lead a small team, exercising sound judgement and initiative to deliver outcomes aligned with organisational priorities. You will be capable of managing competing priorities, providing authoritative and well-reasoned advice, and representing the work area with stakeholders. Many roles involve people leadership responsibilities, including setting direction, managing workflows, developing staff capability and monitoring performance.


    This recruitment stream covers a range of specialist roles. Individual positions may focus on data analytics, geospatial analysis, market analysis, economic and policy analysis, research, reporting or data management, and candidates are not expected to possess expertise across all disciplines.


    Depending on the role, duties may include:


    • Provide authoritative written and verbal advice to senior stakeholders by translating complex economic and policy analysis, spatial patterns, or research findings into clear options, recommendations, implications and risks.
    • Lead very complex data, geospatial, market intelligence, policy or economic analysis activities under broad direction, exercising judgement, independence and accountability for quality outcomes.
    • Set priorities, work plans and performance expectations for a team, project or analytical work program; monitor delivery through others and adjust priorities as risks, demand or evidence changes.
    • Lead complex problem solving by integrating evidence from internal and external datasets, spatial information, scheme or operational data, research and stakeholder intelligence.
    • Oversee governance, assurance and decision quality for analytical products, including data standards, metadata, peer review, traceability, auditability, privacy, secrecy, ethical use of data and defensible documentation.
    • Manage and influence key stakeholder relationships, including other government agencies, users of analysis, data custodians, technical specialists, policy and compliance teams, market bodies or other external partners, to support sound decision-making and implementation.
    • Drive innovation and business improvement across analytics, geospatial, reporting, market intelligence, data management or market monitoring processes, including use of tools, automation, quality controls and improved end-user products.
    • Manage team capacity, capability and wellbeing; coach staff, provide performance feedback and build capability in analytical reasoning, GIS/spatial methods, economic and market analysis, data governance and communication of insights.
    • Identify and manage risks for the work program, including information security, data quality, analytical integrity, reputational risk, market sensitivity, delivery risk and appropriate escalation and controls.


    Specific roles and responsibilities will be discussed with candidates as they progress through the recruitment process. If an offer of employment is made to you, you will receive the detailed duties for the specific role being offered.

    The Organisation

    The Australian Security Intelligence Organisation (ASIO) protects Australia and Australians from threats to their security. ASIO’s people are ordinary Australians but they do extraordinary things – they are our most important asset. To be successful, we need talented people who are highly capable, dedicated, adaptable and resilient.


    We seek to reflect the community we protect. ASIO is committed to fostering a diverse and inclusive environment, where all staff are valued and respected. We welcome applications from all eligible candidates, irrespective of gender, sexual orientation, ethnicity, religious affiliation, age or disability. Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples are encouraged to apply. We are secretive about what we do, not what we value.


    At a time when Australia’s security environment is dynamic, diverse and degrading, our work is more important than ever. Join the mission and make a difference every day.

    The opportunity

    ASIO is seeking proactive, motivated and passionate Business Analysts. You will contribute to ASIO’s mission through working with project managers, technical experts and business stakeholders at all levels to investigate, analyse, validate, and document business, functional and non-functional requirements. You will guide technology change through all stages of the project lifecycle and play a key role in transitioning ASIO staff to new ways of working.


    You will have the ability to drive project requirements from inception to delivery. As a pivotal part of cross-functional multi-disciplinary teams, you will use a broad range of business analysis techniques to:

    ·        Define business problems

    ·        Understand business teams’ current state

    ·        Define future state

    ·        Design and prototype user interfaces

    ·        Document business, functional and non-functional requirements

    ·        Lead user acceptance testing

    ·        Support technical and business teams through implementation and adoption.


    A merit pool may be created to fill future vacancies which have the same or similar requirements to this position. This merit pool will be valid for up to 18 months.
    jobDuties

    Responsibilities

    As a Business Analyst, you will:

    ·        Investigate business needs, problems and opportunities to support and recommend effective business and ICT changes.

    ·        Develop artefacts to support the requirements definition such as requirement specifications, user stories, process models, operational concepts, prototypes and data driven insights.

    ·        Build relationships and work collaboratively with stakeholders by facilitating workshops,

    stakeholder interviews, presentations, product backlog management and user acceptance

    testing.

    ·        Contribute to and manage activities for planning, development, enhancement and

    implementation of projects and systems.

    ·        Identify issues, risks and benefits of current and proposed solutions and apply expertise to

    detail the resulting impacts.

    ·        Lead and support business transition to embed change.

    ·        Continually develop the business analysis competency within the Organisation.


    ITE2 Business Analyst (APS6 equivalent): The ITE2 role will be capable of working under broad direction to perform and achieve a range of moderately complex ICT business analysis work in a variety of contexts.


    SITEC Senior Business Analyst (EL1 equivalent): The SITEC role will be capable of working under limited direction to perform and achieve highly complex ICT business analysis work in a wide and sometimes unpredictable range of contexts. This is a leadership role and may include line management of staff.
    jobEligibilityRequirements

    Location

    These positions are located in Canberra. Relocation assistance is provided to successful candidates where ASIO requires you to relocate.

    We advertise positions nationally to reach the broadest audience. Please refer to the above location before applying.


    How to apply

    ASIO officers’ identities are protected by law. Under section 92 of the ASIO Act, the only declared ASIO officers are the Director-General and Deputy Directors-General. Due to the need to protect our people’s identities, ASIO holds all employment applications in the strictest of confidence. It is essential that you do the same. Please do not discuss your application with others as doing so may adversely affect your potential employment.


    Click on ‘Apply online’ to commence your application. Your application must be complete and include the following:

    ·        A written pitch of up to 500 words that addresses the required capabilities for the role. Use examples to demonstrate how your skills and experience meet the capabilities.

    ·        A current CV, no more than 2 pages in length, outlining your employment history, the dates and a brief description of your role, as well as any academic qualifications or relevant training you have undertaken.

    ·        Details of 2 referees, which must include a current or former supervisor. ASIO will not contact your referees without advising you prior to doing so.


    Before applying read the ASIO People Capability Framework and Core Capability Guide, both available on the ASIO website. To increase your chances of success, tailor your application responses to align with the relevant capabilities and levels outlined in these frameworks.


    Use of artificial intelligence in recruitment processes

    ASIO does not use any form of artificial intelligence (AI) at any point in the recruitment process.

    All applications are reviewed by a selection panel. We recognise some applicants may use AI technologies to draft applications or prepare for interviews. If you wish to use AI, we recommend doing this only for initial drafts and ensure that your application is personalised and accurately reflects your skills, experience and qualifications.


    Additionally, be aware of the privacy policies of any AI tool you use. Be mindful of the information you are entering into AI tools (personal details, work history, etc) and how using it may link you to potential ASIO employment, noting that you are expected to be discrete and maintain confidentiality with your application.


    If you are invited to attend interview, the use of AI tools and recording devices during interview is strictly prohibited. Should AI tools or recording services be detected, your application, and any future applications may be discontinued.

    The recruitment selection process

    Our selection process is rigorous and extensive. All employment decisions and selection processes at ASIO are based on merit principles and candidates must be prepared to undergo various selection stages. The stages may include application assessment, eligibility checks, online testing, skills-based assessment and interview.


    We ask all applicants for their patience throughout the process. 

    Employment conditions

    Employment is under the Australian Security Intelligence Organisation Act 1979. Conditions of service are similar to those applying in the Australian Public Service. We recognise entitlements accrued under the Public Service Act 1999 and provide for continuation of superannuation under the Commonwealth schemes. Salary packaging arrangements are also available.

    Australian Workplace Equality Index

    ASIO has achieved the Australian Workplace Equality Index (AWEI) Gold Standard in 2024, 2025 and 2026, recognising ASIO’s inclusion work and positive culture. ASIO was the first Australian intelligence agency to achieve this status.

    For more information about ASIO, please visit: www.asio.gov.au.

    About the CDPP


    The CDPP is Australia’s federal independent prosecution service established by Parliament to prosecute alleged offences against Commonwealth law. We aim to provide an effective, ethical, high quality and independent criminal prosecution service for the nation in accordance with the Prosecution Policy of the Commonwealth. 


    The CDPP is a small, agile federal government agency that offers a dynamic working environment focused on important legal outcomes, with ten offices located across Australia. More details can be found at the CDPP website.


    The Enabling Services Group (ESG) supports the work of the CDPP national litigation practices, providing a range of corporate functions managed by highly qualified and experienced APS professionals.


    The CDPP offers a range of attractive conditions; whether you are looking for interesting work impacting on the Australian community or flexible work arrangements and attractive work entitlements. For more details please review the CDPP Enterprise Agreement.

    About the CDPP


    The CDPP is Australia’s federal independent prosecution service established by Parliament to prosecute alleged offences against Commonwealth law. We aim to provide an effective, ethical, high quality and independent criminal prosecution service for the nation in accordance with the Prosecution Policy of the Commonwealth. 


    The CDPP is a small, agile federal government agency that offers a dynamic working environment focused on important legal outcomes, with ten offices located across Australia. More details can be found at the CDPP website.


    There are five litigation practice groups at the CDPP which conduct criminal prosecutions in nominated crime types across Australia. One of these practice groups, Legal Capability and Performance (LCP) focuses on building legal capability and providing legal support functions for criminal prosecutions.


    The CDPP offers a range of attractive conditions; whether you are looking for interesting work impacting on the Australian community or flexible work arrangements and attractive work entitlements.


    LCP employees have a mixture of legal and non legal expertise, with all employees engaged under the CDPP Enterprise Agreement.


    Please note that the Prosecutor Allowance does not apply to all LCP roles, regardless of the employees legal expertise and qualifications.
